时间
该研究涵盖从1998年1月1日到2025年12月31日的28年时间,研究发表日期为2026年11月24日。
地点与对象
该研究是全球性的,涉及 130 个国家和地区,重点关注儿科人群。美国、中国和日本是贡献最多的国家,其中美国在出版量方面领先。
事件
对 2,161 篇有关儿科 诺如病毒 感染的出版物进行了全面的文献计量分析,揭示了研究成果和合作网络的趋势。Bioengineer.org 将这项研究作为科学评论进行了报道。
规模、影响与风险
Norovirus is responsible for over 700 million infections and an estimated 200,000 deaths annually, with children under five experiencing the highest incidence and mortality rates. It accounts for 63.20% of foodborne viral infections globally.
应对、状态与不确定性
目前没有特定的 抗病毒药物 药物或保护性 疫苗 被批准用于临床。该研究强调了进一步研究和持续开发基因组追踪和 疫苗 设计方法的必要性。

HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, November 25, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Study Maps 28 Years of Pediatric Norovirus Research Across Global Networks by November 24, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 6 mins read 0 Study Maps 28 Years of Pediatric Norovirus Research Across Global Networks Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram A comprehensive bibliometric analysis tracking nearly three decades of scientific literature reveals that global research into pediatric norovirus infections has transitioned from early taxonomic identification toward sophisticated genomic tracking, disease burden quantification, and vaccine design. Norovirus is widely recognized as a leading viral cause of acute gastroenteritis and diarrheal outbreaks across the globe, ranking as the fifth leading cause of disability-adjusted life years within the global burden of disease. Globally, the pathogen accounts for more than 700 million infections and an estimated 200,000 deaths annually, with children under five years of age experiencing both the highest incidence rates and the highest mortality burdens. Primarily transmitted through the fecal-oral route, norovirus is responsible for approximately 63.20 percent of foodborne viral infections worldwide, spreading rapidly through contaminated water supplies, shellfish, fresh fruits, vegetables, and ready-to-eat food products. Despite the widespread public health consequences of these recurrent outbreaks, no specific antiviral medications or protective vaccines are currently approved for clinical use, prompting an expanding body of specialized scientific inquiry. To rigorously examine how the international scientific landscape around childhood norovirus infections has developed, investigators retrieved literature published between January 1, 1998, and December 31, 2025, from the Clarivate Analytics Web of Science Core Collection Science Citation Index Expanded database. The bibliographic search, executed on April 1, 2026, targeted English-language original research articles and reviews using structured topical terms encompassing norovirus terminology and pediatric populations. The selection strategy identified 2,161 eligible publications after systematic dual-reviewer screening, resolving discrepancies through third-party evaluation to yield an inter-rater reliability score of Cohen’s kappa equal to 0.92. Methodological quality control adhered to the preliminary BIBLIO guidelines across core operational dimensions, including search protocols, eligibility benchmarks, data cleaning, and synthesis standards. Data were processed using three complementary computational mapping platforms: CiteSpace version 6.4.R1 Advanced, VOSviewer version 1.6.20, and the R-based Bibliometrix scientific mapping package, which together enabled multi-angle visualizations of temporal trends, international co-authorship networks, co-citation links, and keyword bursts. Discover more Tour Colleges Study Biology Study Chemistry The quantitative trajectory of pediatric norovirus scholarship demonstrated continuous expansion over the 28-year observation window, dividing into three distinct quantitative stages. Between 1998 and 2004, the field resided in its formative infancy, characterized by modest annual publication numbers that rose gradually from six papers in 1998 to 24 papers in 2004. An intermediate period of steady acceleration occurred from 2005 through 2013, during which yearly scholarly outputs expanded progressively from 33 to 83 articles as international recognition of pediatric norovirus burden intensified. Research activity increased significantly between 2014 and 2025, with annual publications crossing the 100-paper milestone for the first time in 2014 at 122 articles and remaining consistently elevated thereafter. Scholarly output peaked in 2021 with 150 published papers, the highest annual total observed in the study, before settling into an annual volume consistently exceeding 100 papers through 2025, accompanied by annual citation totals exceeding 5,000 citations in each of the final five consecutive years. Discover more Engineering Job Board Research Paper Access Learn Engineering Track Biotech Analysis of national contributions identified 130 participating countries and territories, though scholarly productivity remained concentrated among a small cadre of leading contributors. The United States produced the largest total volume with 406 publications, followed by China with 307 publications and Japan with 193 publications. Combined, these three nations contributed more than 40 percent of total global research outputs. Cross-border collaboration networks mapped through multi-country collaboration indices highlighted structural variations in international engagement: the United States led globally in multinational collaborative publications and sustained dense co-authorship ties spanning Europe, South America, Oceania, and Asia. Japan maintained a substantial proportion of international collaborative publications, whereas China demonstrated a lower proportion of multinational co-authored studies despite ranking second in overall productivity. Furthermore, citation analysis revealed that publications originating from the United States, Australia, and Germany attained the highest average citations per paper, reflecting strong global scientific utilization of findings generated by these research communities. At the institutional level, 2,882 distinct organizations contributed to pediatric norovirus research, organizing into seven main institutional collaboration clusters that reflect both longstanding foundations and emerging investigative centers. The University of Tokyo ranked first globally in institutional publication volume with 84 articles, followed by Emory University with 66 papers and Chiang Mai University with 62 papers. Among high-output institutions, Baylor College of Medicine accumulated the greatest total citations. Network timeline mapping illustrated that historical hubs represented by the University of Tokyo provided foundational stability throughout the earliest phases of the discipline. In contrast, institutions such as Emory University expanded output rapidly during middle and later stages to become central anchors of recent collaborative networks. Concurrently, newer nodes such as Chang Gung University and the Evandro Chagas Institute emerged as newer participants in the broader collaborative framework, demonstrating a multi-tiered structural evolution across geographic regions over time. Individual investigator analysis identified 1,049 active researchers across nine primary co-authorship clusters, confirming an interconnected multi-group research architecture rather than an isolated community dominated by a single team. The most prolific authors across the 28-year timeline were Jan Vinje with 81 publications, Hiroshi Ushijima with 80 publications, and Pattara Khamrin with 59 publications, supported by high-output investigators primarily based in the United States and Japan. Co-citation analysis, which tracks how frequently researchers are referenced together by third-party studies, identified Robert L. Atmar with 485 co-citations, Lisa C. Lindesmith with 483 co-citations, and Manish M. Patel with 471 co-citations as the foundational intellectual figures in pediatric norovirus science. The co-citation network grouped these prominent scholars into four interconnected clusters, illustrating how basic laboratory virology, mucosal immunology, and clinical field trials converged to support modern translational pediatric gastroenteritis research. Pediatric norovirus research appeared across 412 academic journals, spanning virology, pediatric infectious diseases, public health, and general medicine. The Journal of Medical Virology led global publishing volume with 194 articles, followed by the Journal of Clinical Virology with 85 articles and PLOS ONE with 76 papers. The top ten publishing outlets were primarily dedicated to virology and infectious diseases, with Clinical Infectious Diseases recording the highest journal impact factor at 8.3 and eight of the top ten journals ranking in Quartile 2 or higher within Journal Citation Reports. Journal co-occurrence networks resolved into three distinct disciplinary groupings: a pathogenic virology cluster centered on the Journal of Medical Virology; a clinical and pediatric medicine cluster anchored by the Pediatric Infectious Disease Journal and Clinical Infectious Diseases; and an open-access, interdisciplinary cluster led by PLOS ONE and Viruses-Basel. Journal citation burst monitoring revealed an early concentration of basic virological emergence in titles such as Archives of Virology, which was later succeeded by prolonged citation bursts in clinical infectious disease periodicals. Keyword analysis revealed a pronounced tripartite conceptual evolution across the 28-year timeline, categorizing 11 distinct thematic keyword clusters into three broad subject domains. High-frequency keywords were dominated by core terms including norovirus with 1,053 occurrences, children with 934 occurrences, gastroenteritis with 725 occurrences, infection with 576 occurrences, and acute gastroenteritis with 525 occurrences. Burst-detection algorithms divided the timeline into an early phase from 1998 to 2010, an intermediate phase from 2010 to 2018, and a recent phase from 2018 to 2025. In the early period, keyword bursts centered on diagnostic detection and taxonomic nomenclature, led by terms such as Norwalk-like virus, round-structured virus, and human calicivirus. The middle period shifted decisively toward molecular epidemiology, driven by the emergence of reverse transcription polymerase chain reaction protocols and intense study of genetic diversity in circulating GII.4 lineage variants. In the recent period from 2018 to 2025, bursts shifted toward public health intervention, featuring terms such as developing countries, burden, rotavirus vaccine, and impact. Literature co-citation mapping further detailed the foundational evidentiary milestones that anchored pediatric norovirus investigations across the 2,161 reviewed publications. The co-citation network of cited references assembled into three major clusters, led by a 2002 investigation by S. Kojima that designed genotype-specific polymerase chain reaction primers, which gathered 295 citations to rank as the most frequently cited foundational paper. Reference burst detection indicated that citation surges began as early as 2001, with two papers sustaining active burst dynamics through 2025. The strongest overall citation burst emerged from a 2019 study authored by P. Chhabra detailing an updated dual-nomenclature classification scheme for norovirus genogroups and genotypes, registering burst intensities ranging up to 77.11. This prominent reference surge began relatively late in the study window and persisted through 2025, underscoring that standardized genomic categorization remains an indispensable technical requirement for contemporary surveillance networks and clinical vaccine trials. The study authors contextualized these findings while outlining structural constraints inherent in bibliometric methodologies. The notable publication peak in 2021 was attributed to the convergence of pandemic-era enteric surveillance adaptations, compensatory publishing backlogs following 2020 operational disruptions, declining costs of whole-genome sequencing, and expanding multicenter surveillance networks. However, several methodological limitations were highlighted: restricting the data source exclusively to the Web of Science Core Collection and English-language publications introduced potential selection and language biases, which may underrepresent critical findings from non-English-speaking, high-burden developing regions. Additionally, publication metrics cannot directly quantify clinical efficacy or real-world policy translation, nor do they capture grey literature, conference abstracts, or governmental surveillance reports. Consequently, the investigators emphasized that future analyses should incorporate multi-database indexing and qualitative policy assessments, while global public health initiatives must strengthen cross-regional partnerships with low-resource nations to achieve equitable, precision-guided norovirus surveillance and control. Time
The study covers a 28-year period from January 1, 1998, to December 31, 2025, with the publication date of the study being November 24, 2026.
Location and population
The research is global, involving 130 countries and territories, with a focus on pediatric populations. The United States, China, and Japan were the top contributors, with the U.S. leading in publication volume.
Event
A comprehensive bibliometric analysis of 2,161 publications on pediatric norovirus infections was conducted, revealing trends in research output and collaboration networks. The study is reported by Bioengineer.org as a scientific review.
Scale, impact and risk
Norovirus is responsible for over 700 million infections and an estimated 200,000 deaths annually, with children under five experiencing the highest incidence and mortality rates. It accounts for 63.20% of foodborne viral infections globally.
Response, status and uncertainty
No specific antiviral medications or protective vaccines are currently approved for clinical use. The study highlights the need for further research and the ongoing development of genomic tracking and vaccine design methods.

Norovirus Activity Rises as Cruise Outbreak Sickens 148 - SuaraGarut.ID
新闻简报A cruise ship outbreak of norovirus sickened 148 passengers and crew members aboard the Caribbean Princess, which departed Fort Lauderdale on November 11 and returned to Port Everglades on November 23, 2026. The CDC confirmed 134 passengers and 14 crew members developed symptoms consistent with norovirus, though the cause remains unknown. The outbreak was reported to the CDC on November 15, 2026, and the vessel followed its scheduled route while isolating affected individuals. Norovirus activity has risen across the United States, with the CDC noting 111 outbreaks between August 1 and October 30, 2026, compared to 109 in the previous season. The virus is responsible for 58% of domestically acquired foodborne illnesses, causing 19 million to 21 million illnesses annually. The CDC recommends thorough handwashing and surface disinfection to prevent transmission, as the virus spreads through contaminated hands, food, and surfaces. The outbreak highlights the importance of effective prevention measures and ongoing surveillance.

暴发于2026年11月15日报告,该船于2026年11月23日返回佛罗里达州埃弗格莱兹港。CDC 监测数据涵盖2026年8月1日至10月30日期间,废水监测显示2026年9月至10月期间活动有所增加。
地点与对象
暴发事件发生在加勒比公主号游轮上,该游轮从佛罗里达州劳德代尔堡出发,返回佛罗里达州埃弗格雷兹港,进行了为期12晚的航程。受影响人口包括2,936名乘客和1,132名机组人员中的148名乘客和机组人员。
事件
诺如病毒旗下一艘游轮暴发造成148名乘客和船员患病。CDC 确认 134 名乘客和 14 名机组人员出现符合 诺如病毒 的症状。暴发 于 2026 年 11 月 15 日向 CDC 报告,但其原因仍不清楚。
规模、影响与风险
The CDC confirmed 111 norovirus outbreaks between August 1 and October 30, 2026, compared with 109 during the same period in the previous season. The outbreak on the Caribbean Princess affected 148 individuals, with 134 passengers and 14 crew members developing symptoms. The virus is responsible for 58% of domestically acquired foodborne illnesses in the United States, causing 19 million to 21 million illnesses annually.
应对、状态与不确定性
公主邮轮在航行期间增加了清洁和消毒程序。当船完成预定航线时,生病的乘客和船员被隔离。CDC 建议用肥皂和流水洗手至少 20 秒,并使用对 诺如病毒 有效的产品对受污染的表面进行消毒。暴发 的原因仍不清楚,CDC 称 监测 数字在正常历史范围内。

Norovirus Activity Rises as Cruise Outbreak Sickens 148 - SuaraGarut.ID

News Brief
A cruise ship outbreak of norovirus sickened 148 passengers and crew members aboard the Caribbean Princess, which departed Fort Lauderdale on November 11 and returned to Port Everglades on November 23, 2026. The CDC confirmed 134 passengers and 14 crew members developed symptoms consistent with norovirus, though the cause remains unknown. The outbreak was reported to the CDC on November 15, 2026, and the vessel followed its scheduled route while isolating affected individuals. Norovirus activity has risen across the United States, with the CDC noting 111 outbreaks between August 1 and October 30, 2026, compared to 109 in the previous season. The virus is responsible for 58% of domestically acquired foodborne illnesses, causing 19 million to 21 million illnesses annually. The CDC recommends thorough handwashing and surface disinfection to prevent transmission, as the virus spreads through contaminated hands, food, and surfaces. The outbreak highlights the importance of effective prevention measures and ongoing surveillance.
Original source text (English)
Norovirus Activity Rises as Cruise Outbreak Sickens 148 Menu Close Up-to-Date Information, Close to the Community. Home Business Lifestyle Tutorials Technology Politics Education Sports Public Info Automotive Opinion Economy Health Temukan lebih banyak Cari Referensi Jasa iklan lokal Olahraga Home Health Norovirus Activity Rises as Cruise Outbreak Sickens 148 Norovirus Activity Rises as Cruise Outbreak Sickens 148 Ashley Morgan Published Nov 24, 2026 - 18:47 2 Reads Last Update Nov 24, 2026 - 18:47 Share WhatsApp English children Smallest Font Largest Font Rising norovirus activity across the United States has coincided with a cruise ship outbreak affecting 148 passengers and crew members, while new research in England reveals substantial infection rates among children. Health surveillance reports and scientific findings highlight the virus's continued transmission and the importance of effective prevention measures. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) confirmed that 134 passengers and 14 crew members aboard the Caribbean Princess developed symptoms consistent with norovirus. The vessel returned to Port Everglades, Florida, on November 23, following a 12-night Caribbean voyage that departed Fort Lauderdale on November 11. The affected individuals represented 2,936 passengers and 1,132 crew members aboard the ship. The outbreak was reported to the CDC on November 15, although its cause remains unknown. Princess Cruises increased cleaning and disinfection procedures during the voyage. Sick passengers and crew members were isolated while the ship completed its scheduled route. Separately, diagnostic company bioMérieux reported increasing norovirus detection rates in all four US regions during November 1-14. The Northeast recorded the highest increase, with positive test rates climbing from 13.4% to 15.7%. The South, Midwest, and West also experienced increases. However, laboratory positivity rates represent results among people tested for gastrointestinal infections and should not be interpreted as the percentage of the entire population currently infected. CDC surveillance recorded 111 norovirus outbreaks between August 1 and October 30, compared with 109 during the corresponding period in the previous season. The agency described the figures as within the normal historical range. The data covered 12 participating states rather than the entire country. Wastewater monitoring provided another indication of changing viral activity. WastewaterSCAN reported an approximately 28% increase in median norovirus genetic material concentrations between September and October, although national measurements remained slightly below levels recorded during the same period a year earlier. October activity was classified as medium. CDC estimates indicate that norovirus causes 19 million to 21 million illnesses annually in the United States. The virus is also responsible for 58% of domestically acquired foodborne illnesses. Meanwhile, research published in PLOS Pathogens examined norovirus GII.4 infection patterns among children in England. Researchers analyzed antibody measurements from 656 children collected between 2007 and 2012, using mathematical modeling to reconstruct exposure patterns across different viral variants. The study estimated an overall infection rate of 205 infections per 1,000 person-years, with a 95% credible interval of 191 to 210. Annual estimates varied considerably, with median rates ranging from 36 to 486 infections per 1,000 person-years. The researchers identified the highest estimated annual attack rate in 2002, corresponding with the emergence of the dominant Farmington Hills variant. Increased infection rates frequently coincided with the appearance of new viral variants, although this relationship was not consistent across every transition. Age-related differences were also evident. Children younger than one year had an estimated infection rate of 161 per 1,000 person-years, with a 95% credible interval of 121 to 210. Among children aged five years and older, the estimate reached 250 per 1,000 person-years, with an interval of 213 to 289. The mathematical model provided moderate evidence for immune imprinting, a process in which the immune system develops stronger antibody responses against viral variants encountered earlier in life. Researchers also found infection estimates substantially higher than previously reported disease-based incidence figures, suggesting that many infections may occur without noticeable symptoms. Norovirus spreads when microscopic particles from infected stool or vomit enter another person's mouth. Contaminated hands, food, bathroom fixtures, and household surfaces can transmit the virus, including through food prepared by someone who is infected. The CDC recommends washing hands with soap and running water for at least 20 seconds. Alcohol-based hand sanitizer should not replace proper handwashing because it does not work effectively against norovirus on its own. People can develop symptoms 12 to 48 hours after exposure, including vomiting, watery diarrhea, and abdominal cramps. Most recover within one to three days, but the virus can remain in stool for two weeks or longer after symptoms disappear. The CDC advises recovered individuals to avoid preparing food or caring for others for at least 48 hours after symptoms end. Contaminated surfaces should be disinfected using products effective against norovirus, while affected clothing and bedding should be washed carefully. Repeated vomiting and diarrhea can cause dehydration, particularly in young children and older adults. Warning signs include reduced urination, dizziness, dry mouth, unusual sleepiness, and crying with few tears in young children. People unable to retain fluids or showing signs of severe dehydration may require medical treatment, including intravenous fluids. Location and population

Event

The outbreak on the Caribbean Princess affected 148 individuals, with 134 passengers and 14 crew members developing symptoms. The virus is responsible for 58% of domestically acquired foodborne illnesses in the United States, causing 19 million to 21 million illnesses annually.

Princess Cruises increased cleaning and disinfection procedures during the voyage. Sick passengers and crew members were isolated while the ship completed its scheduled route. The CDC recommends washing hands with soap and running water for at least 20 seconds and using products effective against norovirus for disinfecting contaminated surfaces. The cause of the outbreak remains unknown, and the CDC described the surveillance figures as within the normal historical range.

流感、RSV 和 诺如病毒 上升,公众敦促“帮助阻止传播” - Pharmacy Business

Flu, RSV and norovirus rise, as public urged to 'help stop the spread' - Pharmacy Business
新闻简报英国卫生安全局 (UKHSA) 报告称,随着冬季临近,英格兰的 诺如病毒、RSV 和流感活动不断增加,敦促公众采取预防措施,防止感染传播。根据监测 2026年11月9日至15日的最新数据,诺如病毒实验室阳性率比同期五个季节平均水平高出5.4%,而轮状病毒活动超出预期水平60.3%。该报告强调了弱势群体脱水的风险,包括幼儿、老年人和免疫功能低下的人。UKHSA 发布了预防感染的指南,例如症状消退后 48 小时内远离工作场所、学校或托儿所,并在此期间避免准备食物。该机构将于 2026 年 11 月 26 日恢复发布医院 诺如病毒 暴发 报告系统 (HNORS) 数据。报告还指出,流感活动正在低水平增加,本季度只有 37% 的两岁和三岁儿童感染了 疫苗 流感。公共卫生官员强调 疫苗接种 和减少这些感染传播的保护措施的重要性。

时间
该报告于2026年11月20日发布。事件日期为2026年11月9日至15日,最新数据涵盖2026年11月2日至15日。
地点与对象
该报告重点关注英格兰,特别关注幼儿、老年人和免疫功能低下的人,他们因 诺如病毒 感染而出现并发症的风险较高。
事件
英国卫生安全局 (UKHSA) 报告称,英格兰的 诺如病毒、RSV 和流感活动不断增加,敦促公众在冬季临近时采取预防措施,防止感染传播。报告还提到,诺如病毒 病例已超过季节性平均水平,轮状病毒活动已超出预期水平。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Flu, RSV and norovirus rise, as public urged to 'help stop the spread' - Pharmacy Business

News Brief
The UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) reported rising activity of norovirus, RSV, and flu in England as winter approaches, urging the public to take precautions to prevent the spread of infections. According to the latest surveillance data from 9–15 November 2026, norovirus laboratory positivity reached 5.4% above the five-season average for the same period, while rotavirus activity exceeded expected levels by 60.3%. The report highlights the risk of dehydration among vulnerable groups, including young children, older people, and immunocompromised individuals. UKHSA has issued guidance on infection prevention, such as staying away from work, school, or nursery for 48 hours after symptoms subside and avoiding food preparation during this time. The agency will resume publishing Hospital Norovirus Outbreak Reporting System (HNORS) data on 26 November 2026. The report also notes that flu activity is increasing at low levels, and only 37% of two and three-year-olds have received their flu vaccine this season. Public health officials emphasize the importance of vaccination and protective measures to reduce the spread of these infections.

With an academic background in forensic science, specialising in forensic chemistry and toxicology, she has laboratory training and research experience in anti-doping and NDPS analysis, toxicological assessments, and criminal investigations. She uses her expertise in scientific research in translating complex developments into insightful, impactful stories. See Full Bio The UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) has reported increasing respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), norovirus and rotavirus activity across England, urging the public to take precautions to prevent infections spreading as winter approaches. According to its latest surveillance report for 9–15 November, RSV activity increased, with laboratory positivity rising to 5.2%, compared with 3.2% in the previous week. Hospital admission rates also increased from 1.29 to 1.66 per 100,000 people. Meanwhile, influenza activity continued to increase at low levels, while Covid-19 activity remained stable at low levels. How are norovirus and rotavirus cases changing? Norovirus activity has increased in recent weeks but remains within expected levels. Laboratory reports between 2 and 15 November were 5.4% higher than the five-season average for the same two-week period. Rotavirus activity has also increased and exceeded expected levels, with reports 60.3% above the five-season average for the corresponding period. UKHSA said the majority (89%) of norovirus samples characterised so far this season belonged to genogroup 2 (GII), with GII.4[P16] being the most frequently identified genotype. Norovirus symptoms include: nausea, vomiting, diarrhoea, abdominal pain, fever and aching limbs. Infection can cause: dehydration, particularly among young children, older people and those who are immunocompromised. Dr Alex Allen, Consultant Epidemiologist at UKHSA, said: “With flu cases creeping up and more young children now having to see their GP due to sickness with flu-like illness, we want to remind parents about the importance of vaccination and protecting young children against flu. Vaccinating all children in school will help to protect them directly and also stop the virus spreading to those at greater risk”. UKHSA has warned that just over a third (37%) of two and three-year-olds have received their flu vaccine this season. What precautions can help prevent infections spreading? Amy Douglas, Lead Epidemiologist at UKHSA, said: “While norovirus remains within expected levels, we’re starting to see a rise in cases and expect this increase to continue as we head into winter. It’s really important that we all take simple steps to help stop the spread”. Douglas added that people experiencing diarrhoea and vomiting should stay away from work, school or nursery until 48 hours after their symptoms have stopped and avoid preparing food for others during this period. 诺如病毒 报告两周内上涨超过 36% - The Pharmacist

Norovirus reports rise by more than 36% in two weeks - The Pharmacist
新闻简报据《药剂师》报道,根据英国卫生安全局 (UKHSA) 的数据,英格兰的 诺如病毒 病例在两周内增加了 36% 以上。监测 2026年11月2日至15日的数据显示,病例数为331例,较前两周增加36.2%。这比同期五个赛季的平均水平高出 5.4%。UKHSA 还指出,轮状病毒活动增加了 60.3%,超出了预期水平。该机构强调,诺如病毒 传播 受社区接触和季节性等因素影响,感染高峰在冬季。他们建议对受污染的表面进行漂白剂消毒,并敦促个人保持手部卫生。该报告强调了 诺如病毒 和流感等其他病原体带来的季节性挑战,这可能会给医疗保健能力带来压力。该调查结果基于已确认的 监测 数据,是 UKHSA 持续监测工作的一部分。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Norovirus reports rise by more than 36% in two weeks - The Pharmacist

News Brief
The Pharmacist reported that norovirus cases in England rose by over 36% in two weeks, according to the UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA). Surveillance data from 2 to 15 November 2026 showed 331 cases, a 36.2% increase compared to the previous two weeks. This is 5.4% higher than the five-season average for the same period. The UKHSA also noted that rotavirus activity increased by 60.3% and exceeded expected levels. The agency emphasized that norovirus transmission is influenced by factors such as community contact and seasonality, with infections peaking in winter. They recommended bleach-based disinfection for contaminated surfaces and urged individuals to maintain hand hygiene. The report highlights the seasonal challenges posed by norovirus and other pathogens like flu, which can strain healthcare capacity. The findings are based on confirmed surveillance data and are part of the UKHSA’s ongoing monitoring efforts.

My Account Log In News Community Pharmacy First Practice Clinical Cancer Cardiovascular Dermatology and wound care Diabetes and endocrinology Mental health and addiction Musculoskeletal and rheumatology Men's health Neurology Nutrition and gastroenterology Paediatrics Pain Respiratory Renal and urology Sexual health Vaccinations and infections Women's health All clinical Clinical Ambassadors All services Insight Newsletter Roundtables Medicines Events Real World General Practice: CVD – March Community Pharmacy and General Practice Conference 2027 | June 27-28 Partners Log in Register News Community Pharmacy First Practice Clinical Cancer Cardiovascular Dermatology and wound care Diabetes and endocrinology Mental health and addiction Musculoskeletal and rheumatology Men's health Neurology Nutrition and gastroenterology Paediatrics Pain Respiratory Renal and urology Sexual health Vaccinations and infections Women's health All clinical Clinical Ambassadors All services Insight Newsletter Roundtables Medicines Events Real World General Practice: CVD – March Community Pharmacy and General Practice Conference 2027 | June 27-28 Partners Home Clinical Vaccinations and infections Norovirus report... Norovirus reports rise by more than 36% in two weeks FG Trade / E+ / via Getty Images Cases of norovirus in England have risen by more than a third in a fortnight, figures from the UK health security watchdog have found. The UK Health Security Agency’s (UKHSA) latest surveillance data, published on 19 November, shows that 331 cases of norovirus were recorded between 2 and 15 November – up 36.2% on the previous two weeks. The total number of cases was 5.4% higher than the average for this period over the past five seasons but activity remained within expected levels, UKHSA said. The agency also said rotavirus activity had increased in recent weeks and exceeded expected levels. The number of rotavirus cases reported between 2-15 November was 60.3% higher than the five-season average for the same period. The UKHSA’s report said: ‘Norovirus transmission is influenced by many factors including, but not limited to, whether the person has been infected with norovirus recently, community contact patterns and the time of the year – infections peak in the colder months and therefore norovirus is most prevalent during winter. More from The Pharmacist Cold weather associated with 2,200 deaths last winter Clinical quiz: managing illness in the returning traveller Wild swimming and waterborne illnesses: a guide for primary care Novel flu and coronavirus infections remain greatest health security threats ‘Transmission is mostly via contact with an infected person, but norovirus can also be spread by consumption of food contaminated with the virus or contact with contaminated surfaces.’ Weldricks Pharmacy said the figures bring hand hygiene back into focus and urged people to wash their hands thoroughly with soap and water for at least 20 seconds after using the toilet or changing nappies and before preparing or eating food. Cleaning products should also be chosen for their suitability against the virus – UKHSA recommends bleach-based disinfection of contaminated surfaces, paying particular attention to toilet seats, flush handles, taps and door handles. Anyone who becomes ill should take small, frequent sips of water to replace lost fluid. A pharmacist can advise whether oral rehydration sachets are appropriate to replace lost water and salts, Weldricks Pharmacy said. It added that most people will recover from norovirus within two to three days, but patients should contact NHS 111 if fluids will not stay down, dehydration persists despite rehydration treatment, or there is bloody diarrhoea. A recent assessment by UKHSA found that seasonal hazards such as flu and norovirus pose challenges for healthcare capacity and health system coordination with the potential for ‘significant’ impact. 英格兰和美国的 诺如病毒 活动有所增加 - cwibc.co.id

Norovirus Activity Rises in England and the US - cwibc.co.id
新闻简报英国和美国的 诺如病毒 活动正在增加,病例数和 暴发 报告均有所增加。在英格兰,英国卫生安全局 (UKHSA) 指出,2026 年 11 月 2 日至 15 日期间,诺如病毒 病例与前两周相比增加了 36.2%。总数也比五个赛季的平均水平高出 5.4%。在美国,2026年8月1日至10月30日期间,CDC记录了111起暴发,仍处于正常的历史范围内。WastewaterSCAN 发现 9 月至 10 月期间 诺如病毒 遗传物质浓度增加了 28%,尽管全国测量值略低于去年的水平。该研究估计总体感染率为每 1,000 人年 205 例感染,每年的估计值差异很大。卫生当局建议用肥皂和水彻底洗手,正确清洁受污染的表面,并使用漂白剂产品进行消毒。从 诺如病毒 中恢复的人应在症状消退后至少 48 小时内避免准备食物或护理。大多数感染会引起呕吐、水样腹泻和腹部绞痛,症状通常在一到三天内消失。脱水可能很严重,尤其是幼儿和老年人,当液体无法保留或出现血性腹泻时,建议就医。
查看新闻五要素
时间
根据 2026 年 11 月的健康 监测 报告,英国和美国的 诺如病毒 活动正在增加,因为寒冷的天气带来了季节性 传播 的增加。英国健康安全局 (UKHSA) 11 月 19 日发布的数据记录了 11 月 2 日至 15 日期间英格兰出现了 331 例 诺如病毒 病例。在美国,诊断公司进行的实验室 监测生物梅里埃还显示,11 月 1 日至 14 日期间,所有四个地区的 诺如病毒 活动均有所增加。
地点与对象
在英国和美国观察到 诺如病毒 活动的增加。在英格兰,英国健康安全局(UKHSA)报告了儿童病例。在美国,疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 在 12 个参与州记录了 暴发。该研究还涉及 2007 年至 2012 年间收集的 656 名英国儿童。
事件
英国和美国的 诺如病毒 活动正在增加,病例数和 暴发 报告均有所增加。UKHSA 指出,与前两周相比,病例增加了 36.2%。CDC 在 8 月 1 日至 10 月 30 日期间记录了 111 例 暴发,而上一季节同期为 109 例。WastewaterSCAN 发现 9 月至 10 月期间 诺如病毒 遗传物质浓度增加了 28%。
规模、影响与风险
UKHSA 报告称,与前两周相比,英格兰的 诺如病毒 病例增加了 36.2%,总数也比五个赛季的平均水平高出 5.4%。在美国,CDC 注意到 111 个 暴发,仍处于正常的历史范围内。WastewaterSCAN 显示 诺如病毒 遗传物质浓度增加了 28%,尽管国家测量值略低于去年的水平。该研究估计总体感染率为每 1,000 人年 205 例感染,每年的估计值差异很大。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生当局强调,仅含酒精的洗手液不足以预防 诺如病毒。他们建议用肥皂和流水洗手至少 20 秒,特别是在使用浴室、更换尿布或清洁污染区域后。被呕吐物或腹泻物污染的表面需要立即清洁和消毒。UKHSA 建议在适当的表面使用漂白剂产品,包括马桶座圈、冲水把手、水龙头和门把手。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Norovirus Activity Rises in England and the US - cwibc.co.id

News Brief
Norovirus activity is rising in England and the US, with increased cases and outbreaks reported. In England, the UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) noted a 36.2% increase in norovirus cases between November 2 and 15, 2026, compared with the previous two-week period. The total was also 5.4% above the five-season average. In the US, the CDC recorded 111 outbreaks between August 1 and October 30, 2026, which remained within the normal historical range. WastewaterSCAN found a 28% increase in norovirus genetic material concentrations between September and October, though national measurements were slightly below last year's levels. The study estimated an overall infection rate of 205 infections per 1,000 person-years, with annual estimates varying substantially. Health authorities recommend thorough handwashing with soap and water, proper cleaning of contaminated surfaces, and the use of bleach-based products for disinfection. People recovering from norovirus should avoid food preparation or caregiving for at least 48 hours after symptoms subside. Most infections cause vomiting, watery diarrhea, and abdominal cramps, with symptoms typically resolving within one to three days. Dehydration can be serious, especially among young children and older adults, and medical advice is recommended when fluids cannot be retained or bloody diarrhea occurs.
Original source text (English)
Norovirus activity is increasing across England and the United States as colder weather brings higher seasonal transmission, according to health surveillance reports covering November 2026. Meanwhile, new research examining English children has identified substantial infection rates and evidence of immune responses shaped by early exposure to different viral variants. Data published by the UK Health Security Agency (UKHSA) on November 19 recorded 331 norovirus cases in England between November 2 and 15. This represented a 36.2% increase compared with the preceding two-week period. The total was also 5.4% above the five-season average for the same period. However, UKHSA said norovirus activity remained within historically expected levels. Rotavirus infections also increased, with reported cases during the same two-week period exceeding the five-season average by 60.3%, according to the agency. UKHSA identified several factors influencing norovirus transmission, including previous infections, patterns of contact within communities, and seasonal conditions. "Norovirus transmission is influenced by many factors including, but not limited to, whether the person has been infected with norovirus recently, community contact patterns and the time of the year – infections peak in the colder months and therefore norovirus is most prevalent during winter. Transmission is mostly via contact with an infected person, but norovirus can also be spread by consumption of food contaminated with the virus or contact with contaminated surfaces." UKHSA said in its surveillance report. In the United States, laboratory surveillance conducted by diagnostic company bioMérieux also showed increasing norovirus activity across all four regions between November 1 and 14. The Northeast recorded the largest increase in positive test rates, rising from 13.4% to 15.7%. These laboratory detection rates reflect people tested for gastrointestinal illnesses rather than the infection rate across the entire population. Nevertheless, separate surveillance findings also indicate ongoing transmission. The US Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) recorded 111 norovirus outbreaks between August 1 and October 30, compared with 109 during the equivalent period of the previous season. The figures came from 12 participating states and remained within the normal historical range. Wastewater surveillance provided another indication of increasing activity. WastewaterSCAN reported that median concentrations of norovirus genetic material increased approximately 28% between September and October, although national measurements remained slightly below levels recorded during the same period last year. CDC national estimates indicate that norovirus causes approximately 19 million to 21 million illnesses annually in the United States. The virus is also responsible for 58% of foodborne illnesses acquired in the country. Health authorities emphasize that alcohol-based hand sanitizer alone provides inadequate protection against norovirus. Washing hands with soap and running water for at least 20 seconds remains the recommended preventive measure, particularly after using the bathroom, changing diapers, or cleaning contaminated areas. Surfaces contaminated by vomit or diarrhea require prompt cleaning and disinfection. UKHSA recommends bleach-based products for appropriate surfaces, including toilet seats, flush handles, faucets, and door handles. Cleaning products should be used according to their instructions, and bleach should never be mixed with other cleaning chemicals. People recovering from norovirus should avoid preparing food or caring for others for at least 48 hours after symptoms disappear, according to CDC guidance. Viral material can continue to be present in stool for two weeks or longer, making careful hygiene important even after recovery. Most infections cause vomiting, watery diarrhea, and abdominal cramps, with symptoms generally developing 12 to 48 hours after exposure. Recovery typically takes one to three days, although dehydration can become serious, particularly among young children and older adults. Weldricks Pharmacy recommends drinking small, frequent amounts of water during illness and consulting a pharmacist about oral rehydration solutions. Medical advice through NHS 111 is recommended when someone cannot retain fluids, continues experiencing dehydration despite rehydration, or develops bloody diarrhea. Additional evidence about the virus comes from a mathematical modeling study published in PLOS Pathogens in 2026. Researchers examined antibody measurements from 656 English children collected between 2007 and 2012 to reconstruct infection patterns involving norovirus GII.4 variants. The model estimated an overall infection rate of 205 infections per 1,000 person-years, with a 95% credible interval of 191 to 210. Annual estimates varied substantially, with median rates ranging from 36 to 486 infections per 1,000 person-years. Age differences were also apparent. Children younger than one year had an estimated infection rate of 161 per 1,000 person-years, compared with 250 per 1,000 person-years among children aged five and older. The researchers identified particularly high infection rates around the emergence of new viral variants. The highest annual estimate occurred in 2002, coinciding with the epidemiological transition to the dominant Farmington Hills variant. The study also found moderate evidence supporting immune imprinting, a hypothesis suggesting that the first norovirus variants encountered early in life can influence subsequent antibody responses. Researchers noted that modeled infection rates exceeded previous estimates based on clinically recognized disease, consistent with substantial asymptomatic transmission

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

胃肠道疾病/诺如病毒 暴发 影响加勒比公主号上的 148 名乘客和船员 - CruiseMapper

GI illness/norovirus outbreak affects 148 passengers and crew aboard Caribbean Princess - CruiseMapper
新闻简报在加勒比海和中美洲为期 12 晚的航行中,加勒比公主号上的 148 名乘客和船员罹患肠胃疾病 暴发。美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 正在监测情况,并就卫生措施和疾病报告提供指导。暴发于11月11日开始,并于11月19日报告,尚未被确认为暴发,尽管它是游轮上此类暴发的常见原因。受影响的人包括134名乘客和14名机组人员,主要症状是呕吐和腹泻。该船已加强清洁和消毒程序,隔离受影响的人员,并收集样本进行检测。暴发 的原因仍未确定,实验室结果仍在等待中。该船计划于11月23日返回劳德代尔堡。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发 于 2026 年 11 月 19 日报道,发生在 11 月 11 日出发、计划于 11 月 23 日返回劳德代尔堡的 12 晚航程中。
地点与对象
暴发 事件发生在加勒比公主号上,这是一艘在加勒比海和中美洲运营的游轮,船上总共 4,068 人(2,936 名乘客和 1,132 名船员)中有 148 人受到影响。
事件
加勒比公主号上报告出现胃肠道疾病 暴发,疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 正在监测情况并提供指导。暴发 尚未确认为 暴发,尽管它是游轮上出现此类 暴发 的常见原因。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 影响了 148 人,其中包括 134 名乘客和 14 名机组人员。呕吐和腹泻是主要症状。这些数字涵盖了整个航程,并不表明所有受影响的人同时患病。原因尚未确定,实验室样本正在等待确认测试。
应对、状态与不确定性
该船增加了清洁和消毒程序,隔离受影响的个人,并收集样本进行实验室检测。疾病预防控制中心正在监测反应情况并指导卫生措施和疾病报告。暴发 的原因仍未确定。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

GI illness/norovirus outbreak affects 148 passengers and crew aboard Caribbean Princess - CruiseMapper

News Brief
A gastrointestinal illness outbreak has affected 148 passengers and crew aboard the Caribbean Princess during its 12-night voyage in the Caribbean and Central America. The U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) is monitoring the situation and providing guidance on sanitation measures and illness reporting. The outbreak, which began on November 11th and was reported on November 19th, has not been confirmed as norovirus, though it is a common cause of such outbreaks on cruise ships. The affected individuals include 134 passengers and 14 crew members, with vomiting and diarrhea being the primary symptoms. The ship has implemented increased cleaning and disinfection procedures, isolated affected individuals, and collected samples for testing. The cause of the outbreak remains undetermined, with laboratory results pending. The vessel is scheduled to return to Fort Lauderdale on November 23rd.
Original source text (English)
CruiseMapper Tracker Ships Ports Lines Deck plans Cabins Accidents Repositioning Cruise Finder News CruiseMapper Cruise news Accidents GI illness/norovirus outbreak affects 148 passengers and crew... GI illness/norovirus outbreak affects 148 passengers and crew aboard Caribbean Princess John Stansfield November 19, 2026 , Accidents A gastrointestinal illness outbreak aboard Caribbean Princess has affected 148 people during a 12-night Caribbean and Central America voyage, according to the U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC). The reported cases include 134 passengers out of 2,936 onboard (4.6%) and 14 crew members out of 1,132 (1.2%). Vomiting and diarrhea were the predominant symptoms. The figures cover the entire voyage and do not indicate that all affected individuals were ill simultaneously. Princess Cruises notified the CDC's Vessel Sanitation Program on November 15th, 4 days after the vessel departed Fort Lauderdale. In response, the ship increased cleaning and disinfection procedures, isolated affected passengers and crew members, and collected samples for laboratory testing. The CDC is monitoring the response and guiding sanitation measures and illness reporting. The cause of the outbreak remains undetermined, with laboratory samples awaiting confirmatory testing. Although norovirus is a common source of gastrointestinal outbreaks aboard cruise ships, it has not been confirmed in this case. Caribbean Princess departed Fort Lauderdale on November 11th for a 12-night itinerary covering destinations in the Dutch Caribbean, Colombia, Panama, Costa Rica and Grand Cayman. The vessel is scheduled to return to Fort Lauderdale on November 23rd. For more Caribbean Princess incidents and accidents, see the ship's CruiseMinus page

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

《独立报》:英格兰的 诺如病毒 病例在短短两周内猛增三分之一

Norovirus cases in England rocket by a third in just two weeks - The Independent
新闻简报Norovirus cases in England have surged by over 35 per cent in the past two weeks, reaching levels 5 per cent higher than typical for this time of year, according to a report by The Independent. The virus, known as the winter vomiting bug, is highly contagious and spreads easily through contaminated surfaces, food, and close contact. Symptoms include sudden nausea, vomiting, and diarrhoea. The article highlights that alcohol-based hand sanitisers are ineffective against norovirus, and recommends thorough handwashing with soap and hot water as the best method of protection. It also advises cleaning contaminated household surfaces with bleach-based products and washing soiled laundry at 60°C or higher. Infected individuals are advised to stay home, rest, hydrate, and avoid preparing food for others until at least 48 hours after symptoms subside. The report does not specify exact case numbers or provide details on the investigation or containment measures taken.
查看新闻五要素
时间
Norovirus cases in England increased by over 35 per cent in the past two weeks, reaching levels around 5 per cent higher than typical for this time of year. The event date is not explicitly stated, but the increase is reported as occurring within the two-week period preceding the publication date of 2026-11-24.
地点与对象
据报道,暴发 发生在英格兰,但没有提及具体地区或社区。受影响人群包括感染 诺如病毒 的个人,但没有提供进一步的人口统计详细信息。
事件
《独立报》的报道证实了这一点。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 的规模被描述为病例显着增加,但没有提供具体数字。虽然没有明确说明风险,但该病毒具有高度传染性,很容易通过受污染的表面、食物和密切接触传播。
应对、状态与不确定性
该文章提供了一般预防建议,例如用肥皂和热水彻底洗手,以及使用漂白剂产品清洁受污染的表面。它没有提及具体的调查、测试或隔离措施。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Nearly 100 cases of norovirus reported at Kansas elementary school, officials say - ABC7 Bay Area

News Brief
A norovirus outbreak at Bentwood Elementary School in Olathe, Kansas, has impacted nearly 100 individuals, including 95 students and staff and two family members. Health officials confirmed the outbreak, noting no hospitalizations were reported. The outbreak began in early October and has not seen new cases since. Officials from the Johnson County Department of Health and Environment and Olathe Public Schools stated that they are continuing to monitor the situation to prevent further spread. Norovirus is known for its high contagiousness, especially in school environments, and causes symptoms like vomiting, diarrhea, and stomach pain. While the outbreak is considered over, ongoing collaboration between health officials and the school district remains to ensure the health and safety of students, staff, and families.
Original source text (English)
24/7 LiveSan FranciscoEast BaySouth BayPeninsulaNorth Bay 60° Virus Nearly 100 cases of norovirus reported at Kansas elementary school, officials say No hospitalizations were reported in the outbreak, health officials said. ByKatie Kindelan Thursday, October 22, 2026 Watch ABC News live news stream and get 24/7 latest, breaking news coverage, and live video. A Kansas elementary school suffered an outbreak of norovirus that impacted nearly 100 people, health officials said. The outbreak at Bentwood Elementary School in Olathe, Kansas, began in early October and spread to 95 cases of norovirus among students and staff and two cases among family members, officials with the Johnson County Department of Health and Environment and Olathe Public Schools told ABC News in a statement Thursday. Two of the cases were confirmed through laboratory testing, with the remaining cases being "epidemiologically linked, meaning individuals experienced similar symptoms around the same time and in the same setting," officials said. Norovirus is a highly contagious virus that is the most common cause of viral gastroenteritis, an inflammation of the inside lining of the gastrointestinal tract. According to school and county health officials, people impacted by the outbreak at Bentwood Elementary experienced symptoms including vomiting, diarrhea, nausea and stomach pain. No hospitalizations were reported, and no new cases have been reported since Oct. 13, officials said. "Throughout the outbreak, JCDHE worked closely with school officials to monitor illnesses, provide public health guidance and help prevent further spread," the school district and health department said in a joint statement. "Although the outbreak is considered over, JCDHE and the Olathe Public Schools will continue to monitor the situation and work together to help protect the health of students, staff and families." What to know about norovirus Norovirus is the leading cause of foodborne illness in the U.S., causing 58% of those illnesses each year, according to the CDC. Top Stories AccuWeather Forecast: Flooding from king tides continue; Level 2 storm with strong winds overnight 9 people shot at a private central California concert; 1 in critical condition, sheriff says Appeals court says federal law requiring accommodations for pregnant workers is unconstitutional Annually, around 21 million Americans are diagnosed with norovirus. The most common symptoms for norovirus are nausea, vomiting, stomach pain and diarrhea. Patients can also experience fever, headaches and body aches. Norovirus is incredibly contagious and frequently reported in school settings, where children interact in close quarters. Most people recover within 1 to 3 days but can still spread the virus for a few days after. The most effective way to prevent the spread of norovirus is hand washing with soap and water, according to the CDC. Hand sanitizer does not kill norovirus. Current CDC data show that cases of norovirus are at medium levels nationally, without significant spikes. Report a correction or typo Copyright © 2026 ABC News Internet Ventures. Korean health agency warns of rising norovirus risk - Food Safety News

News Brief

疑似 诺如病毒 病例的乘客获准从游轮下船 - 开曼指南针 - 金博宝

Passengers cleared to disembark from cruise ship with suspected norovirus cases - Cayman Compass
新闻简报大开曼岛公共卫生官员在评估报告的疑似 诺如病毒 病例大幅下降后,于 2026 年 11 月 20 日允许加勒比公主号游轮上的健康乘客下船。该船此前曾出现胃肠道疾病病例增加的情况,怀疑是由诺如病毒引起的。随着感染防控措施的落实,病例数下降,健康乘客可以安全下机。官员强调,保护居民和游客的健康是他们的首要任务。该评估包括公共卫生 监测 官员和环境卫生部港口卫生官员的审查。该决定是基于既定的公共卫生协议和加勒比公共卫生局的指导。没有明确报告死亡或严重的健康影响,重点仍然是控制疾病和确保安全。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 11 月 20 日,该船抵达大开曼岛后,公共卫生官员对情况进行了评估。据报道,在实施感染控制措施后,病例数有所下降。
地点与对象
抵达大开曼岛的加勒比公主号游轮上的乘客身体健康,已获准下船。受影响的人群包括船上的乘客和船员。

公共卫生官员证实,在实施感染控制措施后,报告的疑似 诺如病毒 病例数量显着下降,允许健康乘客下船。

公共卫生官员对报告的病例以及为控制疾病所采取的措施进行了评估。他们确定健康的乘客可以按照既定协议安全下机。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Passengers cleared to disembark from cruise ship with suspected norovirus cases - Cayman Compass

News Brief
Public health officials in Grand Cayman cleared healthy passengers from the Caribbean Princess cruise ship to disembark on 20 November 2026 after assessing a significant decline in reported cases of suspected norovirus. The ship had previously experienced an increase in gastrointestinal illness cases, which were suspected to be caused by norovirus. Following the implementation of infection prevention and control measures, the number of cases dropped, allowing for the safe disembarkation of healthy passengers. Officials emphasized that protecting the health of residents and visitors was their priority. The assessment included a review by a public health surveillance officer and the port health officer from the Department of Environmental Health. The decision was based on established public health protocols and guidance from the Caribbean Public Health Agency. No deaths or severe health impacts were explicitly reported, and the focus remained on managing the illness and ensuring safety.
Original source text (English)
Facebook Instagram Linkedin X Youtube News Issues Opinion Editorial Letters to the Editor Sports Community Storm Centre Archives Classifieds Job Adverts More Daily Newsletter Jobs Health Travel E-versions Special Features and Publications Team Submit a Story Idea Submit Letter to the Editor Watch/Listen Video Radio Z99 Rooster 101.9 Gold FM 94.9 Island FM 98.9 Freedom of Information in the Cayman Islands Advertise With Us Online Media Kit Print Media Kit Classifieds Rate Sheet Pay Bill Legal Help & FAQ Contact Search Search Cayman Compass Compass TV Compass Radio News Issues Opinion Editorial Letters to the Editor Sports Community Storm Centre Archives Classifieds Job Adverts More Daily Newsletter Jobs Health Travel E-versions Special Features and Publications Team Submit a Story Idea Submit Letter to the Editor Watch/Listen Video Radio Z99 Rooster 101.9 Gold FM 94.9 Island FM 98.9 Freedom of Information in the Cayman Islands Advertise With Us Online Media Kit Print Media Kit Classifieds Rate Sheet Pay Bill Legal Help & FAQ Contact Search Home Featured Local Home Page Passengers cleared to disembark from cruise ship with suspected norovirus cases Health Local Passengers cleared to disembark from cruise ship with suspected norovirus cases By Staff - 21 November 2026 Share Facebook X WhatsApp Linkedin ReddIt Email Public Health officials said reported cases of suspected norovirus aboard the Caribbean Princess declined significantly after infection-control measures were implemented. - Photo: File The Public Health Department cleared healthy passengers aboard the Caribbean Princess cruise ship to disembark on 20 Nov., after an assessment of reported gastrointestinal illness aboard the vessel. In a press release, Public Health officials said they had been in communication with the ship’s medical team before its arrival in Grand Cayman to review the reported cases and the measures taken to manage the illness. The release stated, “The ship had previously reported an increase in cases of gastrointestinal illness, suspected to be norovirus. Following the implementation of infection prevention and control measures aboard the vessel, the number of reported cases declined significantly.” After the ship arrived, a public health surveillance officer and the port health officer from the Department of Environmental Health boarded the vessel to verify the information provided and assess the public health measures in place. Following the assessment, the Public Health Department determined that “healthy passengers could safely disembark, in accordance with established public health protocols and guidance from the Caribbean Public Health Agency.” - Advertisement - Passengers who were ill, along with their identified close contacts, were required to remain aboard the ship. Chief Medical Officer Dr. Hilary Wolf said, “Cruise ships have established procedures for managing gastrointestinal illness, and our role is to assess the information provided and determine whether disembarkation can proceed safely.” She added, “In this instance, the significant decline in reported cases, together with the measures implemented aboard the vessel and the findings of our assessment, provided the necessary assurance to permit healthy passengers to come ashore.” Medical Officer of Health Dr. Williams-Rodriguez emphasised that protecting the health of residents and visitors remains the priority when determining whether passengers can disembark. Norovirus is a common cause of gastroenteritis, typically resulting in symptoms such as vomiting, diarrhoea and stomach cramps. Most people recover within one to three days. 诺如病毒 在美国再次崛起,一个常见错误可能会让您暴露在危险中 - NCHStats

Norovirus Is Rising Again in the US, and One Common Mistake Could Leave You Exposed - NCHStats
新闻简报文章报告称,诺如病毒 在美国正在增加,2026 年 11 月 1 日至 14 日报告期内,所有四个地区的检测率均较高。东北地区增幅最大,从 13.4% 升至 15.7%。CDC指出,2026年8月1日至10月30日期间出现了111起暴发,这在正常的历史范围内,但这些数字是基于12个参与州的,可能无法反映全国的全部情况。WastewaterSCAN 发现,2026 年 9 月至 10 月期间,诺如病毒 遗传物质增加了 28%,全国活动归类为中等。CDC 警告称,单独使用洗手液并不能有效对抗 诺如病毒,并建议用肥皂和流水洗手至少 20 秒。诺如病毒 可以在症状出现之前传播,个人应在症状停止后 48 小时内避免准备食物。该病毒会引起突然呕吐和腹泻,很容易传播,并可能导致严重脱水。文章强调了洗手和适当消毒对于预防 传播 的重要性,尤其是在浴室等共用空间。

时间
该文章发表于2026年11月20日。活动日期范围为2026年11月1日至14日,最新的CDC 监测更新和废水报告为2026年9月至10月。

规模、影响与风险
Millions of Americans get norovirus every year, with the CDC estimating 19 million to 21 million illnesses annually. It causes sudden vomiting and diarrhea, spreads easily between family members, and can lead to serious dehydration. WastewaterSCAN reported a 28% increase in norovirus genetic material between September and October 2026, with national activity classified as medium.
应对、状态与不确定性

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Norovirus Is Rising Again in the US, and One Common Mistake Could Leave You Exposed - NCHStats

News Brief
The article reports that norovirus is increasing in the United States, with higher detection rates in all four regions during the November 1 to 14, 2026, reporting period. The Northeast saw the largest rise, from 13.4% to 15.7%. The CDC noted 111 outbreaks between August 1 and October 30, 2026, which is within the normal historical range, but these figures are based on 12 participating states and may not reflect the full national situation. WastewaterSCAN found a 28% increase in norovirus genetic material between September and October 2026, with national activity classified as medium. The CDC warns that hand sanitizer alone is not effective against norovirus, and recommends washing hands with soap and running water for at least 20 seconds. Norovirus can spread before symptoms appear, and individuals should avoid food preparation for 48 hours after symptoms stop. The virus causes sudden vomiting and diarrhea, spreads easily, and can lead to serious dehydration. The article emphasizes the importance of handwashing and proper disinfection to prevent transmission, especially in shared spaces like bathrooms.
Original source text (English)
Skip to content North American Community Hub Statistics Home US Population News Blog Demographics Disease Data Hospital Data Birth and Fertility Mortality U.S Maps Cities Population States More About Us Editorial Policy Contact Us Home US Population News Blog Demographics Disease Data Hospital Data Birth and Fertility Mortality U.S Maps Cities Population States More About Us Editorial Policy Contact Us Search Norovirus Is Rising Again in the US, and One Common Mistake Could Leave You Exposed Robert McAllister Published: November 20, 2026 Updated: November 20, 2026 Disease Data, News Norovirus is showing up more frequently in laboratory tests across the United States as the country enters its winter illness season. The latest results show increases in all four US regions, with the Northeast reporting the highest detection rate. Millions of Americans get norovirus every year. It causes sudden vomiting and diarrhea, spreads easily between family members and can leave someone seriously dehydrated. One common mistake can make it easier to catch. Many people rely on hand sanitizer to protect themselves, but the CDC warns that sanitizer alone does not work well against norovirus. Washing with soap and running water remains the recommended protection. Table of Contents Toggle Norovirus Rates Are Increasing Across the US Hand Sanitizer Alone Will Not Protect You From Norovirus Norovirus Can Spread Before You Know Someone Is Sick How Long Are You Contagious After Norovirus Symptoms Stop? What Should You Do If Someone in Your House Gets Norovirus? How Can You Tell If It Is Norovirus or Another Stomach Infection? Norovirus Rates Are Increasing Across the US A new report released by diagnostic company bioMérieux found norovirus activity increasing in every US region during its November 1 to 14 reporting period. The Northeast recorded the largest increase, with the detection rate rising from 13.4% to 15.7%. Rates also increased in the South, Midwest and West. The latest laboratory report provides the following figures. Where Are Norovirus Rates Rising the Most? Change in positive test rates across four US regions, November 2026. Northeast 13.4% → 15.7% (+2.3 points) South 12.9% → 13.6% (+0.7 points) Midwest 12.6% → 13.2% (+0.6 points) West 10.2% → 10.6% (+0.4 points) The bars compare the size of each increase. Figures show the share of clinical samples testing positive for norovirus, not the percentage of residents infected. Source: bioMérieux US Medical Affairs Gastrointestinal Trends Report, November 1 to 14, 2026. People tested for stomach infections are a selected group, so their positive test rates cannot tell us how many Americans are currently sick. The CDC reported 111 outbreaks between August 1 and October 30, compared with 109 during the equivalent period last season. The latest CDC surveillance update describes the total as within the normal historical range. Those outbreak figures come from 12 participating states. They do not include every outbreak occurring across the country. Another warning came from wastewater surveillance. WastewaterSCAN reported that the median concentration of norovirus genetic material increased by about 28% between September and October. Its national wastewater measurements remained slightly below the levels recorded at the same time last year. The October wastewater report classified national activity as medium. Hand Sanitizer Alone Will Not Protect You From Norovirus Alcohol-based hand sanitizer has become part of everyday life. People use it after shopping, traveling or touching shared surfaces. Norovirus presents a problem because ordinary hand sanitizers do not work well against it. The virus can remain on your hands after using sanitizer, leaving you exposed when you touch food or put your fingers near your mouth. The CDC recommends washing hands with soap and running water for at least 20 seconds. Handwashing is especially important after using the bathroom, changing diapers or cleaning up after someone who is sick. Wash again before eating or preparing food. Sanitizer can still be used alongside regular handwashing. However, keeping a bottle beside the kitchen sink should never replace washing with soap. Norovirus in Your Home? Here Is What to Do The virus can spread after symptoms disappear. Timing is important. EVERY TIME YOU WASH YOUR HANDS 20 seconds Use soap and running water. Hand sanitizer alone does not work well against norovirus. ↓ WHEN SOMEONE IS SICK Keep Them Away From Food Preparation Clean up vomit or diarrhea immediately and disinfect contaminated surfaces using a product effective against norovirus. ↓ AFTER VOMITING AND DIARRHEA STOP Wait 48 hours Avoid preparing food or caring for other people for at least two full days after the last symptom. ↓ AFTER RECOVERY 2 weeks or longer Norovirus can remain in stool for this long. Continue careful handwashing, especially after using the bathroom. A two-week isolation period is not required. Source: CDC norovirus prevention recommendations. The 48-hour period begins when symptoms have stopped, not when they first appear. Norovirus Can Spread Before You Know Someone Is Sick Norovirus spreads when tiny particles of infected stool or vomit enter another person’s mouth. Contaminated hands, food and household surfaces can carry the virus. Someone preparing a family dinner may infect others without realizing they are carrying norovirus. Food does not need to look spoiled to contain the virus. According to CDC national estimates, norovirus causes 19 million to 21 million illnesses in the United States each year. It is also responsible for 58% of foodborne illnesses acquired in the country. Shared bathrooms deserve attention when someone becomes sick. Toilet handles, faucets and door handles can become contaminated. After a vomiting or diarrhea episode, wear disposable gloves and remove the mess using paper towels. Clean the affected area and follow with a disinfectant suitable for norovirus. The EPA maintains a list of effective disinfectants. Check the product label for the required contact time, since many products must remain wet on a surface for several minutes. The CDC also recommends properly diluted chlorine bleach for suitable surfaces. Never mix bleach with other cleaning products. How Long Are You Contagious After Norovirus Symptoms Stop? Most people begin feeling sick 12 to 48 hours after exposure. Vomiting, watery diarrhea and stomach cramps are common. Symptoms generally improve within one to three days. Feeling better does not mean the virus has disappeared. The CDC advises people to avoid preparing food or caring for others for at least 48 hours after symptoms stop. Norovirus can remain in stool for two weeks or longer after recovery. That creates a particular risk in households with children, where someone who has recovered may quickly return to preparing meals or helping younger family members in the bathroom. Keep washing your hands carefully after recovery. If possible, give the sick person a separate bathroom during the illness and disinfect shared surfaces. What Should You Do If Someone in Your House Gets Norovirus? Start by limiting contact with the sick person and cleaning contaminated surfaces promptly. Avoid sharing towels, and wash bedding or clothing soiled by vomit or diarrhea using hot water and detergent. Handle dirty laundry carefully without shaking it. The sick person also needs enough fluids. Repeated vomiting and diarrhea can quickly cause dehydration, especially in young children and older adults. Small, frequent sips may be easier to manage than drinking a large glass at once. Oral rehydration solutions contain water and salts in measured amounts to replace what the body loses. Keeping oral rehydration packets at home can help when illness begins suddenly. Watch for reduced urination, dizziness, a dry mouth or unusual sleepiness. Young children may cry with very few tears. Anyone unable to keep fluids down or showing signs of serious dehydration needs medical attention. Severe cases may require fluids through an IV. How Can You Tell If It Is Norovirus or Another Stomach Infection? Norovirus typically causes sudden vomiting and watery diarrhea. However, those symptoms also occur with food poisoning caused by bacteria and several other infections. Bloody diarrhea is unusual for norovirus. It can occur with Shigella infection, which may also cause fever and painful stomach cramps. Diarrhea lasting for weeks may point toward another cause, including cyclosporiasis linked to contaminated food. Doctors can use stool testing when symptoms are severe, last longer than expected or suggest an outbreak. Antibiotics do not treat norovirus because it is caused by a virus. There is currently no approved norovirus vaccine, although researchers are testing candidates. Related Posts: Measles Alert After Infected Child Visited... The article discusses norovirus activity across all four US regions: Northeast, South, Midwest, and West. It also mentions the nationwide impact, with the CDC reporting outbreaks in 12 participating states.
Event
Norovirus is showing up more frequently in laboratory tests across the United States during its winter illness season. The CDC reported 111 outbreaks between August 1 and October 30, 2026, which is within the normal historical range.
Scale, impact and risk
Millions of Americans get norovirus every year, with the CDC estimating 19 million to 21 million illnesses annually. It causes sudden vomiting and diarrhea, spreads easily between family members, and can lead to serious dehydration. WastewaterSCAN reported a 28% increase in norovirus genetic material between September and October 2026, with national activity classified as medium.
Response, status and uncertainty
The CDC recommends washing hands with soap and running water for at least 20 seconds, especially after using the bathroom, changing diapers, or cleaning up after someone who is sick. Hand sanitizer alone is not effective against norovirus. The article notes that the outbreak figures come from 12 participating states and do not include every outbreak in the country. The latest CDC surveillance update describes the total as within the normal historical range.

News Brief
A norovirus outbreak has sickened over 100 students and staff at an elementary school in North Carolina, as reported by WBTV. The outbreak was confirmed to have caused gastrointestinal symptoms, with no specific mention of deaths or exposures. The school moved classes online to mitigate the spread of the illness. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

公主游轮上的疾病 暴发 确认为 诺如病毒

Illness Outbreak on Princess Cruise Ship Confirmed as Norovirus
新闻简报公主邮轮加勒比公主号上的病例 暴发 已被 CDC 确认为 诺如病毒。该游轮从英尺出发。佛罗里达州劳德代尔堡于 2026 年 11 月 11 日(预计于 2026 年 11 月 23 日返回)报告称,4.6% 的乘客和 1.2% 的机组人员生病,症状主要是呕吐和腹泻。一旦患病率超过3%,疾病预防控制中心就将暴发正式标记为“暴发”,公主邮轮已采取措施,如加强清洁、隔离患病人员、收集粪便样本进行检测等。船舶卫生计划 (VSP) 正在监测情况,邮轮公司正在遵循其 暴发 预防和应对计划。这是继4月底和5月初发生类似事件后,加勒比公主号今年发生的第二起诺如病毒 暴发事件。暴发 事件是 2026 年公主邮轮上发生的四起 暴发 事件中的一部分,前三起发生在星辰公主号、加勒比公主号和红宝石公主号上。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发于2026年11月21日报道,2026年11月23日更新。该游轮于2026年11月11日出发,预计于2026年11月23日返回。
地点与对象
暴发 事件发生在公主游轮公司的加勒比公主号上,该游轮目前正在从英国堡前往加勒比海、巴拿马运河和哥斯达黎加进行为期 12 晚的航行。佛罗里达州劳德代尔堡。受影响人口包括134名乘客和14名机组人员。
事件
公主邮轮加勒比公主号上的病例 暴发 经 CDC 确认为 诺如病毒。暴发是由邮轮公司向CDC报告的,一旦患病率超过3%,CDC就正式将其标记为暴发。
规模、影响与风险
暴发 影响了 2,936 名乘客和 1,132 名机组人员中的 134 名乘客(4.6%)和 14 名机组人员(1.2%)。主要症状是呕吐和腹泻。CDC已确认病原体为诺如病毒,邮轮公司正在加强清洁和隔离措施。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 已确认 暴发 为 诺如病毒,公主邮轮正在与 CDC 合作,加强清洁、隔离患病乘客并收集粪便样本进行检测。VSP 正在监测情况,邮轮公司已遵循其 暴发 预防和应对计划。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Illness Outbreak on Princess Cruise Ship Confirmed as Norovirus

News Brief
An illness outbreak on the Princess Cruises ship Caribbean Princess has been confirmed as norovirus by the CDC. The cruise, which departed from Ft. Lauderdale, Florida, on November 11, 2026, and is scheduled to return on November 23, 2026, reported that 4.6% of passengers and 1.2% of crew members became ill, with symptoms primarily being vomiting and diarrhea. The CDC labeled the outbreak as official once the illness rate crossed 3%, and Princess Cruises has taken measures such as increased cleaning, isolation of ill individuals, and collection of stool specimens for testing. The Vessel Sanitation Program (VSP) is monitoring the situation, and the cruise line is following its outbreak prevention and response plan. This is the second norovirus outbreak on the Caribbean Princess this year, following a similar incident in late April and early May. The outbreak is part of a series of four norovirus incidents on Princess Cruises ships in 2026, with the first three occurring on Star Princess, Caribbean Princess, and Ruby Princess.
Original source text (English)

travel weather warning Southwest seat upgrade dispute What not to do when deplaning Thanksgiving travel delays Illness Outbreak on Princess Cruise Ship Confirmed as Norovirus Cruise Fever Follow Ben Souza Sat, November 21, 2026 at 8:17 PM UTC Sat, November 21, 2026 at 8:17 PM UTC Update November 23: The CDC has confirmed the illness outbreak that sickened nearly 150 passengers and crew was caused by norovirus. A Princess cruise ship that is currently on a 12-night cruise to the Caribbean, the Panama Canal, and Costa Rica from Ft. Lauderdale, Florida, has reported a gastrointestinal illness (GI) outbreak to the CDC. Caribbean Princess at Port Everglades. Photo: Cruise Fever Three days ago, the ship from Princess Cruises reported to the CDC that more than 3% of guests on board Caribbean Princess have become ill during the cruise. Advertisement Advertisement A passenger on the cruise ship told Cruise Fever that crew members on the ship began taking extra precautions on the ship when 2% of guests became sick. This included extra sanitation and no more self-service at the buffet. Once that number crossed 3%, the CDC officially labeled it as an illness outbreak. So far, 134 of 2,936 (4.6%) of passengers and 14 of 1,132 (1.2%) of crew members have reported being sick during the sailing. It is important to note that these numbers are for the entire cruise so far; it does not mean that they were all sick at the same time. The CDC has reported that the causative agent was norovirus, with predominant symptoms among guests and crew members being vomiting and diarrhea. Advertisement Advertisement Due to the outbreak on the ship, Princess Cruises is working with the CDC, and they have performed the following actions: Increased cleaning and disinfection procedures according to their outbreak prevention and response plan Collected stool specimens from gastrointestinal illness cases for testing Isolated ill passengers and crew Consulted with VSP about sanitation cleaning procedures and reporting ill cases The Vessel Sanitation Program (VSP) is remotely monitoring the situation. This includes reviewing the cruise ship's response and sanitation procedures. Caribbean Princess. Photo: Cruise Fever This is the second illness outbreak on the cruise ship this year. Back in late April and early May, a norovirus outbreak spread to 160 guests and crew members. Advertisement Advertisement This is the fourth outbreak on a Princess ship this year. The first three were caused by norovirus on Star Princess, Caribbean Princess, and Ruby Princess. Cruise lines are required to report to the VSP anytime 2% or more of passengers/crew members report ill on a voyage. If the number crosses 3%, it is officially labeled as an outbreak and listed on the CDC's website. Caribbean Princess departed from Port Everglades on November 11, 2026, and is scheduled to return to the port on November 23, 2026. The cruise had scheduled port stops in Aruba, Colombia, Panama, Costa Rica, and Grand Cayman. The post Illness Outbreak on Princess Cruise Ship Confirmed as Norovirus appeared first on Cruise Fever. The outbreak was reported on November 21, 2026, with an update on November 23, 2026. The cruise departed on November 11, 2026, and is scheduled to return on November 23, 2026.
Location and population
The outbreak occurred on the Princess Cruises ship Caribbean Princess, which is currently on a 12-night cruise to the Caribbean, the Panama Canal, and Costa Rica from Ft. Lauderdale, Florida. The affected population includes 134 passengers and 14 crew members.
Event
An illness outbreak on the Princess Cruises ship Caribbean Princess was confirmed as norovirus by the CDC. The outbreak was reported to the CDC by the cruise line, and the CDC officially labeled it as an outbreak once the illness rate crossed 3%.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak affected 134 passengers (4.6%) and 14 crew members (1.2%) out of a total of 2,936 passengers and 1,132 crew members. The predominant symptoms were vomiting and diarrhea. The CDC has confirmed the causative agent as norovirus, and the cruise line is implementing increased cleaning and isolation measures.
Response, status and uncertainty
The CDC has confirmed the outbreak as norovirus, and Princess Cruises is working with the CDC to implement increased cleaning, isolate ill passengers, and collect stool specimens for testing. The VSP is monitoring the situation, and the cruise line has followed its outbreak prevention and response plan.
