因 诺如病毒 住院的成人经常面临严重的肾脏和代谢并发症 - Bioengineer.org

Adults Hospitalized With Norovirus Frequently Face Severe Renal And Metabolic Complications - Bioengineer.org
新闻简报Bioengineer.org 于 2026 年 11 月 20 日发表的一项研究强调了美国因 诺如病毒 住院的成年人面临的严重肾脏和代谢并发症。这项研究于 2022 年 7 月至 2024 年 6 月期间进行,分析了 Optum 临床数据集市的全国行政索赔数据,确定了 192,430 起全因 急性胃肠炎 病例和 2,589 起 诺如病毒 特定住院病例。研究结果显示,57.2% 的全因胃肠炎病例存在代谢紊乱,而 62.0% 的 诺如病毒 住院病例也经历过类似问题。急性肾损伤也很常见,影响全因病例的 38.3% 和 诺如病毒 病例的 42.6%。全因病例中脱水和败血症的比例分别为 29.2% 和 26.5%,诺如病毒 病例中分别为 41.9% 和 28.1%。该研究强调,这些并发症不仅限于老年人,因为年轻人也经常面临严重后果。该研究旨在提高对 诺如病毒 住院期间高危人群的临床和公共卫生了解。
查看新闻五要素
时间
该研究于2022年7月至2024年6月期间进行,新闻于2026年11月20日发布。
地点与对象
该研究主要针对美国住院成人,中位年龄为 74 岁,其中很大一部分患者年龄在 60 岁及以上。
事件
一项描述性研究评估了全国行政索赔数据,以评估成人中与 急性胃肠炎 和 诺如病毒 编码的住院相关的临床并发症,揭示了严重的肾脏和代谢问题。
规模、影响与风险
在全因 急性胃肠炎 住院治疗中,57.2% 患有代谢紊乱,38.3% 患有急性肾损伤,29.2% 患有脱水,26.5% 患有败血症。对于 诺如病毒 编码的住院治疗,62.0% 患有代谢紊乱,42.6% 患有急性肾损伤,41.9% 患有脱水,28.1% 患有败血症。
应对、状态与不确定性
该研究以行政索赔数据为基础,并使用标准 ICD-10-CM 诊断 代码进行识别。研究结果是描述性的,旨在帮助临床医生和研究人员识别高危人群。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Adults Hospitalized With Norovirus Frequently Face Severe Renal And Metabolic Complications - Bioengineer.org

News Brief
A study published on November 20, 2026, by Bioengineer.org highlights the severe renal and metabolic complications faced by adults hospitalized with norovirus in the United States. The research, conducted between July 2022 and June 2024, analyzed nationwide administrative claims data from Optum’s Clinformatics Data Mart, identifying 192,430 episodes of all-cause acute gastroenteritis and 2,589 norovirus-specific hospitalizations. The findings revealed that 57.2% of all-cause gastroenteritis cases had metabolic disturbances, while 62.0% of norovirus-coded hospitalizations experienced similar issues. Acute kidney injury was also common, affecting 38.3% of all-cause cases and 42.6% of norovirus cases. Dehydration and sepsis were documented in 29.2% and 26.5% of all-cause cases, respectively, and 41.9% and 28.1% of norovirus cases. The study emphasized that these complications are not limited to older adults, as younger adults also faced high frequencies of severe outcomes. The research aimed to improve clinical and public health understanding of at-risk populations during norovirus hospitalizations.
Original source text (English)
HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER Wednesday, November 25, 2026 No Result View All Result Login HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER HOME NEWS EXPLORE CAREER Companies Jobs Lecturer PhD Studentship Postdoc Research Assistant EVENTS iGEM News Team PHOTOS VIDEO WIKI BLOG COMMUNITY FACEBOOK INSTAGRAM TWITTER No Result View All Result No Result View All Result Home NEWS Science News Health Adults Hospitalized With Norovirus Frequently Face Severe Renal And Metabolic Complications by November 20, 2026 in Health Reading Time: 3 mins read 0 Adults Hospitalized With Norovirus Frequently Face Severe Renal And Metabolic Complications Share on FacebookShare on TwitterShare on LinkedinShare on RedditShare on Telegram Adults admitted to hospitals in the United States with acute gastroenteritis frequently experience severe clinical complications, including metabolic disturbances and acute kidney injury. According to a descriptive study evaluating nationwide administrative claims data between July 2022 and June 2024, these serious systemic, renal, and metabolic conditions affect both all-cause gastroenteritis cases and patients with specific norovirus diagnoses. Norovirus remains recognized as the leading global cause of acute gastroenteritis, yet contemporary research detailing the clinical spectrum of related inpatient hospitalizations has previously remained limited. To better understand these inpatient risks, researchers examined records from Optum’s de-identified Clinformatics Data Mart administrative claims database. The investigative team identified adult patients aged 18 years and older who experienced hospitalized all-cause acute gastroenteritis or norovirus-specified episodes during the two-year observation period. Diagnostic identification relied on standard International Classification of Diseases, Tenth Revision, Clinical Modification diagnosis codes. The investigators focused on clinical complications that emerged during acute hospitalizations occurring within three days of the index hospital episode. The resulting descriptive cohort encompassed 192,430 hospitalized all-cause acute gastroenteritis episodes alongside 2,589 hospitalized norovirus-coded episodes. Overall, the hospitalized patient population was predominantly comprised of older individuals, exhibiting a median age of 74 years. Furthermore, investigators observed that patients within the study cohort frequently presented with multiple documented underlying medical conditions, highlighting an underlying health vulnerability across hospitalized individuals diagnosed with acute gastrointestinal illness. Discover more Study Biology Soothe Digestion Check Symptoms Study Anatomy Find Treatments Across all-cause acute gastroenteritis hospitalizations, metabolic disturbances represented the single most frequent complication, documented in 57.2 percent of episodes. Acute kidney injury followed as the second most common complication, diagnosed in 38.3 percent of cases. Dehydration was recorded in 29.2 percent of episodes, while systemic sepsis developed in 26.5 percent of hospitalizations, illustrating that gastrointestinal admissions frequently involve substantial physiological decompensation beyond localized digestive symptoms. Patients hospitalized specifically with norovirus-coded episodes experienced even higher frequencies across these major clinical complications. Metabolic disturbances affected 62.0 percent of norovirus-coded hospitalizations, and acute kidney injury was documented in 42.6 percent of these patients. In addition, dehydration occurred in 41.9 percent of the norovirus cohort, while sepsis was identified in 28.1 percent of admissions, establishing that acute norovirus infection often coincides with profound renal and metabolic strain. The researchers analyzed outcomes across specific age groups, dividing patients into younger adults aged 18 to 59 years and older adults aged 60 years and older. In addition to the heavy burden observed among older patients, the authors reported that high frequencies of complications were also observed among younger adults hospitalized with norovirus. This pattern indicates that severe clinical consequences from norovirus illness are not confined solely to geriatric inpatient populations. For patients aged 60 years and older, baseline frailty was formally measured using a validated claims-based frailty index. The assessment demonstrated that baseline frailty was substantially higher among older adults who had underlying medical conditions compared to those without comorbid illnesses. Ultimately, the authors concluded that hospitalized adults with all-cause and norovirus acute gastroenteritis commonly face severe renal, metabolic, and systemic complications, helping clinicians and public health researchers better identify populations at greatest risk. Norovirus outbreaks impact hundreds of Lincoln elementary students

News Brief
Norovirus outbreaks have significantly impacted Lincoln elementary schools, keeping hundreds of students away from classes in recent weeks. The Lincoln Journal Star reported the issue on November 19, 2026, and updated it on November 21, 2026. The outbreak is described as affecting multiple schools, with no specific case or death counts provided. The article highlights the need for ongoing investigation and contact tracing, though no detailed measures or outcomes are mentioned. The source is a local newspaper, and the information is current as of the update date. The outbreak is attributed to norovirus, which is a common cause of gastroenteritis, and the situation remains under active monitoring.

公主游轮上的疾病 暴发 确认为 诺如病毒 - Cruise Fever

Illness Outbreak on Princess Cruise Ship Confirmed as Norovirus - Cruise Fever
新闻简报据 Cruise Fever 报道,公主游轮上的 暴发 疾病已被确认为 诺如病毒 感染。暴发 于 2026 年 11 月 23 日被识别,但证据中未提供有关病例数、死亡数或暴露数的具体细节。该报告没有提及任何官方应对措施,例如测试、隔离或接触者追踪,也没有具体说明游轮的位置。暴发 被报告为已确认事件,但由于所提供证据中缺乏详细信息,其规模和风险评估仍不清楚。文章强调了疾病的确认为诺如病毒,这是胃肠炎的常见原因,但没有详细说明邮轮公司或卫生当局所采取的影响或行动。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Illness Outbreak on Princess Cruise Ship Confirmed as Norovirus - Cruise Fever

News Brief
An illness outbreak on a Princess Cruise Ship has been confirmed as norovirus infection, according to a report by Cruise Fever. The outbreak was identified on November 23, 2026, but specific details about the number of cases, deaths, or exposures are not provided in the evidence. The report does not mention any official response measures, such as testing, isolation, or contact tracing, nor does it specify the location of the cruise ship. The outbreak is reported as a confirmed event, but the scale and risk assessment remain unclear due to the lack of detailed information in the supplied evidence. The article highlights the confirmation of the illness as norovirus, which is a common cause of gastroenteritis, but does not elaborate on the implications or actions taken by the cruise line or health authorities.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

加勒比游轮 Sickens 148–诺如病毒 疑似 - La Voce di New York

Caribbean Cruise Sickens 148–Norovirus Suspected - La Voce di New York
新闻简报A gastrointestinal illness outbreak has been suspected on the Caribbean Princess cruise ship, operated by Princess Cruises, during its 12-night voyage through the Caribbean and Central America. As of November 15, 2026, 134 passengers and 14 crew members have reported symptoms of vomiting and diarrhea, representing 4.6% of passengers and 1.2% of crew members. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) is investigating the cause, with no confirmed diagnosis yet. The cruise line has implemented health protocols, including isolating symptomatic individuals and disinfecting common areas. However, the exact pathogen responsible remains unidentified, and laboratory results are pending. The CDC is monitoring the situation through its Vessel Sanitation Program, and the ship’s return to Florida is still scheduled. Health experts emphasize the importance of frequent handwashing and rapid isolation of symptomatic individuals to prevent further spread.
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发于2026年11月15日报告,在11月11日出发、计划于11月23日结束的12晚航程中的几天内记录到了疾病。
地点与对象
加勒比公主号是公主邮轮公司运营的一艘游轮,航线穿越加勒比海和中美洲,停靠阿鲁巴岛、哥伦比亚、巴拿马、哥斯达黎加和大开曼岛。它载有 2,936 名乘客和 1,132 名机组人员。
事件
据美国疾病控制与预防中心(CDC)报告,胃肠道疾病 暴发 疑似由加勒比公主号游轮上的 诺如病毒 引起。该邮轮公司启动了健康协议以遏制疾病的传播。
规模、影响与风险
134名乘客和14名机组人员出现呕吐和腹泻,分别占乘客的4.6%和机组人员的1.2%。CDC 正在通过其船舶卫生计划监控 暴发,但原因尚未得到证实。
应对、状态与不确定性
邮轮公司已对有症状的乘客和船员进行隔离,船上也进行了消毒。收集了生物样本以鉴定病原体,但实验室结果仍在等待中。疾病预防控制中心正在远程监控情况,目前尚未宣布该船的返回时间表有任何变化。

Caribbean Cruise Sickens 148–Norovirus Suspected - La Voce di New York

News Brief
A gastrointestinal illness outbreak has been suspected on the Caribbean Princess cruise ship, operated by Princess Cruises, during its 12-night voyage through the Caribbean and Central America. As of November 15, 2026, 134 passengers and 14 crew members have reported symptoms of vomiting and diarrhea, representing 4.6% of passengers and 1.2% of crew members. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) is investigating the cause, with no confirmed diagnosis yet. The cruise line has implemented health protocols, including isolating symptomatic individuals and disinfecting common areas. However, the exact pathogen responsible remains unidentified, and laboratory results are pending. The CDC is monitoring the situation through its Vessel Sanitation Program, and the ship’s return to Florida is still scheduled. Health experts emphasize the importance of frequent handwashing and rapid isolation of symptomatic individuals to prevent further spread.
Original source text (English)
Founded by Stefano Vaccara Subscribe for only $6/Year Login Editor in Chief: Giampaolo Pioli america Oggi 1988 The First Italian English Digital Daily in the US English Editor: Grace Russo Bullaro English Edition News New York U.N. People Entertainment Arts Lifestyles Food & Wine Travel Business Sports Italian Edition No Result View All Result English Edition News New York U.N. People Entertainment Arts Lifestyles Food & Wine Travel Business Sports Italian Edition No Result View All Result No Result View All Result in News November 20, 2026 in News November 20, 2026 0 Caribbean Cruise Sickens 148–Norovirus Suspected Illnesses reported aboard the Caribbean Princess as CDC investigates the cause and the ship continues its voyage byFilippo Colle Ph. ANSA Time: 2 mins read A Caribbean vacation turned into a health emergency for 148 people aboard the Caribbean Princess, a cruise ship operated by Princess Cruises. According to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, 134 passengers and 14 crew members experienced vomiting and diarrhea during the 12-night voyage, which departed Fort Lauderdale, Florida, on November 11 and is scheduled to end November 23. The ship is carrying a total of 2,936 passengers and 1,132 crew members. The reported cases represent 4.6% of passengers and 1.2% of crew members. The vessel, which is traveling through the Caribbean and Central America with stops in Aruba, Colombia, Panama, Costa Rica and Grand Cayman, reported the outbreak to U.S. health authorities on November 15. The CDC said the cases were recorded over several days and that not everyone affected became ill at the same time. Passengers isolated and ship disinfected The cruise line immediately activated health protocols aimed at containing the spread of illness, stepping up cleaning and disinfection of common areas and isolating passengers and crew members who showed symptoms. Biological samples were also collected in an effort to identify the pathogen responsible for the gastrointestinal illness. “Most guests have not been affected, and we continue to closely monitor the situation,” Princess Cruises told Fox News, confirming that it had strengthened preventive measures. The CDC is monitoring the outbreak remotely through its Vessel Sanitation Program, the federal program responsible for overseeing health and sanitation conditions on cruise ships. So far, no changes have been announced to the ship’s scheduled return to Florida. A previous outbreak in May and the possibility of norovirus Health authorities have not yet identified the cause of the outbreak. One possibility is norovirus, a highly contagious virus that causes vomiting and diarrhea and can spread quickly in shared environments, but laboratory results are not yet available. The Caribbean Princess was already involved in a similar outbreak between April and May 2026, when 145 passengers and 15 crew members became ill during another cruise. In that case, testing confirmed norovirus as the cause. The latest episode adds to a series of gastrointestinal outbreaks reported aboard cruise ships this year. Health experts stress that frequent handwashing and the rapid isolation of people with symptoms remain essential tools for limiting the spread of infections. To determine the source of this latest outbreak, however, authorities will have to wait for the test results. 诺如病毒是12月健康讲座的明确选择:职业医生的理由和推荐的主题顺序

Norovirus Is the Clear Choice for December's Health Talk: An Occupational Physician's Reasons and the Recommended Order of Topics
新闻简报该文章发表于 2026 年 11 月 23 日,建议将 诺如病毒 作为 12 月健康讲座的主要主题,强调其在 12 月至 1 月 暴发 季节的相关性。作为一名职业医生,作者强调,诺如病毒 是一个关键课题,因为它是一个高峰季节,需要全面的预防和应对策略,而且它发生在所有类型的工作场所。文章概述了健康讲座的五部分结构,涵盖预防、呕吐物清理程序、消毒剂准备、重返工作岗位指南和常见问题。它强调,用肥皂和流水洗手是根本,而酒精消毒剂的效果较差。作者还指出,法律上没有规定感染 诺如病毒 的员工必须休息,工作场所应制定自己的重返工作岗位标准。本文没有报告任何已确认的 暴发 或具体统计数据,但提供了准备工作的一般指导。
查看新闻五要素
时间
文章发表于2026-11-23,活动与12月暴发季节相关,即12月至1月。
地点与对象
该讨论适用于任何工作场所,包括办公室、医院等,不限于特定地点或人群。
事件
文章推荐 诺如病毒 作为 12 月健康讲座的主题,理由是其 暴发 季节、预防和应对的全面覆盖以及与所有工作场所的相关性。这是建议,而不是已确认的 暴发 或事件。
规模、影响与风险
这篇文章没有报告具体的病例数、死亡人数或暴露人数。它讨论了工作场所中 诺如病毒 的一般风险以及做好准备的必要性。
应对、状态与不确定性
本文提供了准备 诺如病毒 的指导,包括预防方法、呕吐物清理程序和重返工作岗位的标准。它没有报告任何正在进行的调查或已确认的 暴发。

Norovirus Is the Clear Choice for December's Health Talk: An Occupational Physician's Reasons and the Recommended Order of Topics

News Brief
The article, published on November 23, 2026, recommends norovirus as the primary topic for December's health talks, emphasizing its relevance during the outbreak season from December to January. As an occupational physician, the author highlights that norovirus is a critical subject due to its peak season, the need for comprehensive prevention and response strategies, and its occurrence in all types of workplaces. The article outlines a five-part structure for the health talk, covering prevention, vomit cleanup procedures, disinfectant preparation, returning-to-work guidelines, and frequently asked questions. It stresses that handwashing with soap and running water is fundamental, while alcohol sanitizers are less effective. The author also notes that there are no legally mandated days off for employees infected with norovirus, and that workplaces should establish their own criteria for returning to work. The article does not report any confirmed outbreak or specific statistics but provides general guidance for preparedness.
Original source text (Japanese)
メインコンテンツへスキップ Translated by AI·Show original Norovirus Is the Clear Choice for December's Health Talk: An Occupational Physician's Reasons and the Recommended Order of Topics かつ先生の健康手帳|産業医・労働衛生コンサルタント Nov 24, 2026 16:51 Have you already decided on the theme for your December health talk? December is a month where you are never short on seasonal topics. Influenza, norovirus, alcohol consumption—any of these can be candidates for a December talk. Among them, what I would recommend first as an occupational physician is norovirus. There are three reasons for this. First, the peak of the outbreak is from December to January. If you discuss it in December, you will have shared it with everyone right at the onset of the outbreak season. Rather than thinking, "I wish I had talked about it back then," after an outbreak occurs, it is far easier for the workplace to discuss it before the peak. Second, you can cover everything seamlessly, from prevention to handling an outbreak. Health talks on influenza tend to end with just prevention. Norovirus is different. After discussing prevention by saying that "handwashing is fundamental," you can transition directly into "what to do that very day if someone vomits at work." It is a topic that allows listeners to leave with an understanding of exact actionable steps for that day. Third, it is an issue that can happen in any workplace. Norovirus is not just an issue for food-handling workplaces or nursing care facilities for the elderly. Even in an office, someone can vomit in the restroom or next to a desk. Workplaces that think, "This doesn't concern us," are the ones that panic the most when it happens. In particular, medical facilities such as the hospital where I work focus heavily on prevention by periodically wiping down environmental surfaces. Recommended 5-Part Order of Topics The order of a health talk should be aligned with the thought process of the listeners, not the number of slides. The recommended structure consists of these five parts. 1. Three Differences from Influenza First, state just three differences from influenza: alcohol sanitizers are less effective; it can be transmitted by an extremely small amount of virus; and there are no legal regulations specifying how many days an employee must take off. Once listeners understand these three points, they will understand why the same measures used for influenza cannot prevent it. 2. Handwashing Is the Foundation of Prevention Next is handwashing. Washing with soap and running water is the basis, and alcohol sanitizer is merely a supplement. There is no need to explain this in exhaustive detail here. Make it brief and definitive: "Handwashing is fundamental; alcohol is not a substitute." If posters are displayed in the workplace, simply pointing to their location is sufficient. 3. Vomit Cleanup Procedures This is the highlight of the talk. What kind of gear should the person cleaning up the vomit wear, and in what order should they act? Gently cover with paper towels, pour disinfectant from the outside, wrap inward from the edges, and place into a sealed bag—the specific procedure itself will not be detailed in this article. That is the part to explain while showing diagrams during the talk. It is meaningless if only designated personnel know it; the trick is to present it so everyone present feels that they, too, can take action. 4. How to Prepare Disinfectant Solutions Disinfectant solutions come in two concentration levels: 0.1% for handling vomit and feces, and 0.02% for wiping down surrounding items such as doorknobs and handrails. If using a 5% undiluted product, 20 mL of stock solution in 1 liter of water makes 0.1%, and 4 mL makes 0.02%. Here, too, simply reading off numbers will not get the message across. State the rationale for the distinction first: "Use the stronger concentration for cleaning vomit, and the weaker one for routine wiping." 5. Guidelines for Returning to Work Finally, cover the criteria for returning to work. There is no legally mandated number of days. In practice, the guideline used is "once vomiting and diarrhea have subsided and regular meals can be consumed." Staff at medical institutions follow the same protocol. Even after symptoms resolve, the virus is typically shed in stool for about a week, so make sure to also emphasize thorough handwashing upon returning to work. Only jobs directly involving food handling are treated differently, where workers are reassigned from tasks directly handling food for a while even after symptoms have subsided. These five parts take about 15 minutes. There is no need to talk for a long time. If you follow this order, it will flow into a single, cohesive narrative ranging from prevention to incident response. How to Answer Frequently Asked Questions There are three questions frequently asked after the talk. I will outline the essentials of the answers here. "Does alcohol sanitizer not work at all?" The answer is that it is less effective. Washing hands with soap and running water is fundamental, and alcohol is only a supplement. For disinfecting objects, use sodium hypochlorite. The one thing you must avoid is relying solely on alcohol. "How many days should an employee take off?" There is no legally established number of days. The benchmark is "once symptoms have subsided." That is precisely why workplaces need to establish criteria for returning to work in advance. The less prepared a workplace is, the more disagreement there will be on the day an incident occurs regarding when the person may return. "Are our measures different from workplaces that handle food?" The basic response is the same. The only difference is what happens after symptoms have subsided. For jobs directly handling food, employees are kept away from food for a period even after recovery. In general offices, the primary focus is simply maintaining thorough handwashing. Summary Norovirus is the definitive topic for December's health lecture. Its peak is from December to January. It covers everything seamlessly from prevention to responding when an outbreak occurs, and it is relevant to any workplace. The speaking order consists of five parts: differences from influenza, handwashing, handling vomit, disinfectants, and criteria for returning to work. I have provided the order and key points up to this point for free. All that's left is to simply speak following this sequence. I have prepared this content in a "ready-to-present format" I have prepared a complete kit containing this article's contents in a format ready to use as-is for your health lecture. It includes a total of 12 files, such as 17 lecture slides (PowerPoint), a presentation script, handouts, a display poster, a disinfectant quick-reference chart, and a vomit cleanup kit checklist. Simply swap in your company name and internal policies and print it out to finish your preparations for December's health lecture and health committee meeting. Hello, I'm Dr. Katsu (practicing hospital director, occupational health consultant, and occupational physician). I create "zero-prep, ready-to-present health lecture materials" for companies and "printable self-care resources" for households. Monthly themes are updated in my magazine. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

巴特勒县卫生官员警告废水中存在 诺如病毒 - WLWT

Butler County health officials warn of norovirus reported in wastewater - WLWT
新闻简报据 WLWT 于 2026 年 11 月 20 日报道,巴特勒县卫生官员已就汉密尔顿废水中检测到的 诺如病毒 发出警告。官员强调,虽然大多数人可以在家康复,但严重腹痛、站立时头晕和无法保留液体等症状可能需要就医。诺如病毒 已知会引起恶心、呕吐、腹泻和胃痉挛,脱水是一个重大风险。医生建议如果症状较轻,请联系初级保健医生,但强调在严重的情况下需要及时护理。目前情况正在调查中,卫生当局敦促居民保持警惕,并在需要时寻求医疗帮助。此警告是监控和管理 诺如病毒 在社区中传播的持续努力的一部分。
查看新闻五要素
时间
巴特勒县卫生官员警告废水中存在 诺如病毒。该报告于美国东部时间 2026 年 11 月 20 日上午 8:25 更新。
地点与对象
受影响的人群包括巴特勒县的居民,特别是汉密尔顿的居民,他们因接触废水而面临 诺如病毒 感染的风险。
事件
该事件已得到卫生官员的确认并由 WLWT 进行报道。
规模、影响与风险
诺如病毒 可引起恶心、呕吐、腹泻和胃痉挛。对于大多数人来说,休息和补充水分足以控制疾病,但脱水是一个重要问题,特别是如果个人无法控制水分的话。其他警告信号包括站立时感到头晕或经历严重腹痛。
应对、状态与不确定性
卫生官员敦促人们注意可能需要医疗护理的症状。他们建议如果症状轻微,请致电初级保健医生,但症状严重的人应立即寻求医疗护理。目前情况正在调查中,需要进一步监测。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Butler County health officials warn of norovirus reported in wastewater - WLWT

News Brief
Butler County health officials have issued a warning about norovirus detected in Hamilton's wastewater, as reported by WLWT on November 20, 2026. The officials highlighted that while most people can recover at home, symptoms such as severe abdominal pain, lightheadedness when standing, and inability to retain fluids may necessitate medical attention. Norovirus is known to cause nausea, vomiting, diarrhea, and stomach cramps, with dehydration being a significant risk. Doctors recommend contacting a primary care physician for mild symptoms but emphasize the need for prompt care in severe cases. The situation is under investigation, and health authorities are urging residents to remain vigilant and seek medical help if needed. This warning is part of ongoing efforts to monitor and manage the spread of norovirus in the community.

️ To everyone working in restaurants in winter | Beware of colds, influenza, and norovirus!

News Brief

Original source text (Japanese)
メインコンテンツへスキップ Translated by AI·Show original ❄️ To everyone working in restaurants in winter | Beware of colds, influenza, and norovirus! 日本の飲食店で働こう! Nov 23, 2026 09:52 To all foreign nationals working in Japan Hello! 😊 This is "Let's work in a Japanese restaurant!" As November draws to a close, the number of cold days in Japan is increasing. Depending on the region, it may even snow. Are you used to the winter cold? For those who come from warmer regions like Vietnam, Nepal, or Myanmar, the Japanese winter might feel quite cold. This time, we will talk about winter health management that everyone working in Japanese restaurants should know about. In restaurants, it is important not only to protect your own health but also to protect the health of customers and the people you work with. 1. Be careful of changes in your physical condition during winter When winter arrives, it is necessary to be careful about influenza and infectious gastroenteritis. Especially in restaurants, there are many opportunities to come into contact with many customers and staff. Also, in the kitchen, there are warm places because fire is used. On the other hand, when you go outside, it can be very cold. Please be careful about these temperature differences as well. ✅ Things to be careful about in your daily life ・Get enough sleep ・Eat proper meals ・Drink water frequently ・Wear warm clothes when it is cold ・Wash your hands after returning from outside ・Ventilate the room ・Do not push yourself when you are feeling unwell These are not difficult things to do. However, in the midst of a busy daily life, it is easy to forget them. First, let's take a look at your own lifestyle. 2 What is influenza? Influenza is an infectious disease caused by a virus. In Japan, it is a disease that often spreads in winter. Main symptoms include: ✅ Fever ✅ Cough ✅ Sore throat ✅ Headache ✅ Fatigue ✅ Muscle and joint pain and so on. Symptoms vary from person to person. There are also cases where the fever is not high. ❓ What should you do if you have a fever? First, please contact your company supervisor. Do not force yourself to go to work; instead, report your symptoms and consult with them. Please visit a medical institution if necessary. Also, it is important to get plenty of rest and stay hydrated. There is no need to force yourself to work when you are feeling unwell. Think about your own health and preventing the spread of infection to those around you. 3 Be especially careful of norovirus in restaurants! This is something I really want you to remember. Norovirus is a virus that causes food poisoning and infectious gastroenteritis. You need to be especially careful in winter. The main symptoms are: ・Nausea ・Vomiting ・Diarrhea ・Abdominal pain etc. What if you have diarrhea or vomiting? Do not perform work that involves handling food directly. For example, "My stomach hurts a little, but it's busy today, so I'll go to work." "I have diarrhea, but I don't have a fever, so I'm fine." Making such judgments on your own is dangerous. If a food handler is infected, the infection can spread to customers through the food. If you have symptoms such as diarrhea or vomiting, be sure to report it to your supervisor. Also, even after symptoms subside, viruses may still be shed. When returning to work handling food, follow your company's hygiene management rules and your doctor's instructions. 4 Hand washing is very important In restaurants, you wash your hands many times every day. However, if you do not wash them correctly, dirt and viruses may remain. Especially for norovirus prevention, hand washing with soap and running water is crucial. Key points for hand washing 1 Wet your hands with running water 2 Use soap 3 Wash your palms and the backs of your hands 4 Wash between your fingers 5 Wash your fingertips and around your nails 6 Wash your thumbs and wrists 7 Rinse thoroughly with running water 8 Dry with a clean paper towel or similar In particular, be sure to wash your hands thoroughly after using the restroom and before handling food. Alcohol disinfection alone is not sufficient as a measure against norovirus. Also, even if you are wearing gloves, hand washing is necessary. 5 How to tell your manager when you are feeling unwell? I think there are some people who find it difficult to explain that they are feeling unwell in Japanese. In such cases, please try using the following Japanese phrases. 🗣️ When you have a fever "Good morning. I have a fever, so it is difficult for me to come to work today." 🗣️ When you have a stomachache "I have a stomachache and I have diarrhea." 🗣️ When you have vomited "I have been vomiting repeatedly since last night." 🗣️ When you want to go to the hospital "I am feeling unwell, so I would like to go to the hospital." 🗣️ When you start feeling unwell while at work "Excuse me. I have started feeling unwell. Could I consult with you for a moment?" There is no need to use difficult Japanese. When it started and what kind of symptoms you have. It is important to communicate this. Make sure to check your company's contact methods and deadlines in advance. 6 To all store managers and trainers From here on, I have a request for those of you who manage and train foreign staff. In restaurants, sudden absences can lead to staff shortages. Absences during particularly busy hours have a major impact on store operations. I have also experienced managing store managers and multiple locations, so I understand how difficult that is. However, forcing staff who are feeling unwell to work can lead to food hygiene issues. In particular, you need to be careful about symptoms of diarrhea and vomiting. “Please consult with us early when you are feeling unwell.” Make sure to convey this to your foreign staff in Japanese that is easy for them to understand. It is also important to explain how to contact the store when they are feeling unwell on a regular basis. Staff who are not proficient in Japanese may sometimes be unable to explain their symptoms well. I hope you will consider the necessary response while confirming what the individual is saying. 7 Let's check with a quiz! ❓ Question You are working at a restaurant. You have had diarrhea since this morning. However, today is a busy day at the restaurant. What will you do? A: It's busy, so I will go to work without saying anything B: I will take medicine and cook as usual C: Inform the manager of your symptoms and consult with them about your work. ✅ The correct answer is "C". If you have symptoms such as diarrhea or vomiting, it is important to avoid work that involves handling food directly and to report it to the person in charge. Do not try to make the decision on your own. In conclusion To all foreign nationals working in Japan. The weather is about to get even colder. I am sure there are days when work is busy. Some of you may also be working hard on your Japanese studies. However, if you are not healthy, it becomes difficult to continue working or studying. Protecting your own health also leads to protecting your customers and the colleagues you work with. When you are feeling unwell, do not push yourself and please consult someone early. And to all store managers and trainers. Please create a workplace environment where foreign staff can report feeling unwell with peace of mind. I hope you all stay healthy and well throughout the winter. Thank you for reading until the end! 😊 If you found this article helpful, please give it a "like". Please also share it with your friends and colleagues who work with you. Reference Materials ・Ministry of Health, Labour and Welfare "FY2026 Winter Acute Respiratory Infection (ARI) Comprehensive Measures Q&A" ・Ministry of Health, Labour and Welfare "Q&A on Norovirus" ・Ministry of Health, Labour and Welfare "On the Prevention of Food Poisoning Caused by Norovirus" #飲食店 #体調管理 #インフルエンザ #特定技能 #外国人労働者 #感染症対策 #食品衛生 #ノロウイルス #食中毒予防 #外国人材育成 #飲食店 #体調管理 #インフルエンザ #特定技能 #外国人労働者 #感染症対策 #食品衛生 #ノロウイルス #食中毒予防 #外国人材育成 トップ 健康・医療 病気 Recommended for you

根据美国克利夫兰诊所(Cleveland Clinic)发布的临床健康教育指引,诺如病毒在塑胶、不锈钢等硬质表面上的存活时间可超过2星期。即使是地毯、布艺梳化等软性表面,病毒依然能存活数天至1星期。更棘手的是病毒的“排毒期”,诺如病毒在患者症状消失后粪便仍可持续排出病毒达2星期甚至更久。为减低传播风险,康复者在症状完全消退后的至少48小时内,切勿为他人准备食物或照顾幼童与长者,以免交叉感染。 诺如病毒在患者症状消失后的粪便排毒期可持续2星期甚至更久。(Unsplash图片) 清理呕吐物为何严禁使用地拖? 家中一旦有人突然呕吐,不少人的第一反应是用地拖直接清理,但这是极其危险的做法。香港卫生防护中心(CHP)的官方防范指引中明确警告,切勿以地拖清理呕吐物。由于地拖的纤维难以彻底消毒,来回擦拭会将高浓度的病毒颗粒均匀涂抹至全屋地板。此外,呕吐过程产生的微细气溶胶(Aerosols)会在空气中短暂悬浮,清理时若无妥善防护,易被口鼻吸入导致感染。 家中一旦有人突然呕吐,不少人的第一反应是用地拖直接清理,但这是极其危险的做法。(AI生成图片) 正确的紧急清理4步骤 1. 穿戴防护:清理前务必戴上即弃手套与外科口罩,并安排其他家庭成员(尤其是长者与幼童)远离受污染区域。 清理前务必戴上即弃手套与外科口罩,并安排其他家庭成员(尤其是长者与幼童)远离受污染区域。(AI生成图片) 2. 由外向内吸除:使用大量即弃纸巾或吸水力强的抹布,从污物边缘向中心小心抹走呕吐物或粪便,切忌大力擦拭导致污物飞溅。 3. 密封弃置:清理完成后,立即将沾有污物的纸巾与手套等放入垃圾袋并紧密扎好,确认完全密封后才弃置。 清理完成后,立即将沾有污物的纸巾与手套等放入垃圾袋并紧密扎好,确认完全密封后才弃置。(AI生成图片) 4. 消毒并保持通风:清走污物后,必须使用1:49稀释家用漂白水彻底消毒受污染表面,期间应打开窗户,确保室内空气流通。 漂白水比例与停留时间有何讲究? 清走污秽物后,消毒是关键一步。美国疾控中心(CDC)的预防指引强调,含氯漂白水能有效消灭诺如病毒。针对不同程度的污染,各地卫生机构制定了精准的漂白水稀释与停留时间标准。 1.一般家居表面(1:99比例):门柄、电灯开关、水龙头、座厕冲水掣等经常接触的家居表面,应使用1:99稀释家用漂白水擦拭。 2.严重污染区域(1:49比例):地面或直接沾染呕吐物及排泄物的浴室范围,须将漂白水浓度提升至1:49。 3.电子产品表面:手机屏幕、平板电脑及遥控器等,可用适用于屏幕的消毒湿纸巾擦拭,以免损坏装置。 门柄等日常高频接触物体的表面,应使用1:99稀释家用漂白水擦拭。(AI生成图片) 将稀释漂白水涂抹至物体表面后,不能立刻用清水抹洗。美国疾控中心要求,漂白水必须在表面停留至少5分钟;香港卫生防护中心则建议静置15至30分钟,确保病毒结构彻底瓦解。时间一到,再用清水抹拭并抹干。 床单、衣物与布艺梳化如何彻底消毒? 诺如病毒同样会依附在患者的睡衣、被褥与毛巾上。根据香港卫生防护中心(CHP)的消毒指引,受污染的衣物与床单在清除污物后,可先在1:49稀释漂白水中浸泡30分钟,再彻底清洗。 受污染的衣物与床单在清除污物后,可先在1:49稀释漂白水中浸泡30分钟,再彻底清洗。(AI生成图片) 若衣物材质无法承受漂白水,可直接放入洗衣机,并设定“最长洗衣程序”,配合洗衣液与60°C以上热水进行清洗。处理时应戴上手套,切勿在空气中大力抖动,以免病毒颗粒扬起扩散。衣物洗净后立即放入干衣机,以“最高温度设定”烘干,利用高温物理灭活病毒。无法机洗的布艺梳化或厚重地毯,建议使用高温蒸气清洁机(Steam cleaner)进行深层热力消毒。 清洁完毕后还有哪些防疫盲点? 大扫除结束后,所有可重复使用的清洁工具,应浸泡在1:49稀释漂白水中30分钟,冲洗并自然风干后才可再次使用。在整个清洁与康复期间,全家人洗手应改用番枧与流动清水搓洗至少20秒,放弃依赖酒精搓手液。 在整个清洁与康复期间,全家人洗手应改用番枧与流动清水搓洗至少20秒,放弃依赖酒精搓手液。(AI生成图片) 克利夫兰诊所医生高德曼(Matthew Goldman)在评估家庭传播风险时直言:“遏止诺如病毒扩散的关键在于严格的卫生习惯,规律洗手、使用漂白水消毒,以及患者在有症状期间避免为他人准备食物,这三项措施缺一不可。” 2026年收纳潮流退烧!微分类越理越乱 盘点7大华而不实收纳地雷洗衣粉洗马桶3个月不用刷?拆解真相:随时蚀坏水箱恐花巨款修缮早晨如厕撞时间?人妻立“6S浴室规矩”惹议 附4招化解告别塞车公屋交吉杂物多难清理 住户:找谁帮较平? B routine priorityPublic-health news

Nearly 150 sick aboard South Florida cruise ship with mystery gastrointestinal illness - KOKH

News Brief
A cruise ship, the Caribbean Princess, departed from Port Everglades in Fort Lauderdale, Florida, on November 11, 2026, for a 12-night Panama Canal cruise. Nearly 150 passengers and crew members, including 134 passengers and 14 crew members, have reported gastrointestinal symptoms such as vomiting and diarrhea. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) was informed about the outbreak on November 15, 2026, and has collected samples for testing. However, the cause of the illness remains unknown. Princess Cruises has taken measures to increase cleaning and isolate affected individuals. The CDC continues to monitor the situation, and officials have noted that the 148 cases represent illnesses reported throughout the trip, not necessarily people sick at the same time. The cruise is expected to return to Fort Lauderdale on November 23, 2026.

According to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention, 148 people aboard the Caribbean Princess have reported vomiting, diarrhea, and other gastrointestinal symptoms. The Princess Cruises ship departed Port Everglades in Fort Lauderdale on November 11 for a 12-night Panama Canal cruise. The outbreak was reported to the CDC on November 15. Of the 4,068 people aboard, 134 passengers and 14 crew members have reported becoming sick. The cause remains unknown. 加勒比公主号上有 148 人生病,因为船舶记录了 2026 年第二次 暴发

148 Sickened on Caribbean Princess as Ship Logs Second Outbreak of 2026
新闻简报加勒比公主号游轮上的134名客人和14名船员患胃肠道疾病暴发,主要症状是呕吐和腹泻。该船于11月11日从劳德代尔堡出发,计划于11月23日返回埃弗格雷斯港,目前正在接受调查以确定原因。卫生官员正在等待实验室结果以确认致病病原体,因为 暴发 是该游轮 2026 年发生的第二起。疾病预防控制中心此前曾报告公主邮轮上出现多起 暴发,其中包括 5 月份的 暴发 暴发,导致 160 人患病。虽然目前的暴发尚未确认与诺如病毒相关,但邮轮公司已采取加强清洁和隔离患病人员等措施。确切的原因仍未解决,随着测试的继续,预计会有进一步的更新。
查看新闻五要素
时间
暴发于2026年11月19日报告,该船的航程于11月11日出发,计划于11月23日返回。
地点与对象
暴发 事件发生在加勒比公主号游轮上,该游轮在加勒比地区运营。总共 2,936 名乘客和 1,132 名船员中,有 134 名乘客和 14 名船员受到影响。
事件
加勒比公主号报告出现胃肠道疾病暴发,以呕吐和腹泻为主要症状。原因尚未得到证实,卫生官员正在等待实验室结果。

应对、状态与不确定性
公主邮轮已实施额外的清洁和消毒措施,并将生病的乘客和船员分开。发病原因仍在调查中,预计该船将在病因确认前返回大沼泽地港。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

148 Sickened on Caribbean Princess as Ship Logs Second Outbreak of 2026

News Brief
A gastrointestinal illness outbreak has affected 134 guests and 14 crew members aboard the Caribbean Princess cruise ship, with vomiting and diarrhea as the primary symptoms. The ship, which departed Fort Lauderdale on November 11 and is scheduled to return to Port Everglades on November 23, is currently under investigation to determine the cause. Health officials are awaiting lab results to confirm the pathogen responsible, as the outbreak is the ship's second of 2026. The CDC has previously reported multiple outbreaks on Princess Cruises ships, including a norovirus outbreak in May that sickened 160 people. While the current outbreak is not yet confirmed as norovirus-related, the cruise line has taken measures such as enhanced cleaning and isolating sick individuals. The exact cause remains unresolved, and further updates are expected as testing continues.

Subscribe More forecasts: Atlanta 30 day forecast Miami/ Crime & Emergencies 148 Sickened on Caribbean Princess as Ship Logs Second Outbreak of 2026 By Elena Vega Published on November 19, 2026 Source: Gareth James / Wikimedia Commons A gastrointestinal illness outbreak has swept through Caribbean Princess, sickening 134 of the 2,936 guests and 14 of the 1,132 crew members aboard, with vomiting and diarrhea reported as the predominant symptoms. The cause has not yet been confirmed, and health officials are still waiting on lab results to figure out exactly what's making people sick. According to CruiseHive, the causative agent for the outbreak has not yet been confirmed, and stool specimens are being collected for testing. The illness cases amount to 4.6% of guests and 1.2% of crew members aboard the ship, the outlet reports. Caribbean Princess is currently on a 12-night voyage that departed Fort Lauderdale on Wednesday, November 11, and the ship is scheduled to return to Port Everglades on Monday, November 23. This isn't the first time this year that Caribbean Princess has dealt with a shipboard illness outbreak. Per the same CruiseHive report, the vessel experienced an earlier outbreak in May that sickened a total of 160 guests and crew members and was attributed to norovirus — making the current situation the ship's second such outbreak of 2026. A Pattern Across the Princess Fleet The November outbreak follows a string of similar incidents aboard Princess Cruises ships this year. USA Today reported in May that the CDC had logged four cruise gastrointestinal illness outbreaks meeting its public-notification threshold so far in 2026, two of which were caused by norovirus. That same May voyage, which ran from April 28 to May 11, saw 102 guests and 13 crew members fall ill, the outlet noted, describing vomiting and diarrhea as the main symptoms. By July, the tally had grown. Cruise Law News reported that a norovirus outbreak aboard Ruby Princess during its June 12 to July 2 Alaska voyage out of San Francisco had sickened 102 passengers and 23 crew members as of July 1, bringing the total number of CDC-reported cruise ship GI outbreaks in 2026 to seven at that point. The outlet described the Ruby Princess incident as the third CDC-reported norovirus outbreak on a Princess Cruises ship that year — more than any other cruise line — and noted that the earlier May Caribbean Princess outbreak had sickened 160 people while a March outbreak aboard Star Princess sickened 193. Star Princess and the Response to Earlier Outbreaks The Star Princess incident was especially severe. Gastroenterology Advisor reported that 193 people fell ill during that voyage with norovirus, including 141 passengers and 52 crew members. In response, the outlet said, Princess Cruises carried out extra cleaning and disinfecting throughout the ship and kept sick passengers and crew members separated from others. A CDC Vessel Sanitation Program page tied to the May Caribbean Princess outbreak lists a snapshot dated May 11 showing 145 of 3,116 passengers and 15 of 1,131 crew members reporting illness at that time — figures that differ somewhat from the earlier case counts reported elsewhere, underscoring how outbreak numbers can shift as a voyage progresses and more cases are logged. A separate CDC listing confirms a Ruby Princess outbreak dated July 6, though it does not include specific case numbers. Conflicting Counts on Norovirus's Role Reporting on how often norovirus drives these outbreaks industry-wide isn't entirely consistent. USA Today reported that norovirus caused 18 of 23 CDC-reported cruise gastrointestinal illness outbreaks in 2025, while Cruise Law News put the figure at 17 of 23 outbreaks that year. Both figures come from otherwise credible reporting, and the discrepancy remains unresolved in available coverage. For now, the exact cause of the illness aboard Caribbean Princess remains unknown while testing continues. The ship is due back in Port Everglades in just under a week, and it remains to be seen whether the cause will be identified before then. Miami-Crime & Emergencies Crime & Emergencies in ... The outbreak occurred on the Caribbean Princess cruise ship, which is operating in the Caribbean region. It affected 134 guests and 14 crew members out of a total of 2,936 guests and 1,132 crew members.
Event
A gastrointestinal illness outbreak was reported on the Caribbean Princess, with vomiting and diarrhea as the main symptoms. The cause has not yet been confirmed, and health officials are awaiting lab results.
Scale, impact and risk
The outbreak affected 4.6% of guests and 1.2% of crew members. While the exact cause remains under investigation, the outbreak is described as the ship's second of 2026 and part of a pattern across the Princess Cruises fleet. The health officials are still waiting on lab results to determine the causative agent.
Response, status and uncertainty
Princess Cruises has implemented extra cleaning and disinfecting measures and separated sick passengers and crew members. The cause of the illness is still under investigation, and the ship is expected to return to Port Everglades before the cause is confirmed.
